Chapter 4: What is Kubernetes? — The Big Picture                     🟢 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%)
    🟢 BEGINNER NOTE: Kubernetes has a reputation for being complex. In this
    chapter, we use the SHIPPING PORT ANALOGY: Control Plane = Harbor
    Master's office, Worker Nodes = Docks, Pods = Ships, Containers =
    Cargo. Every K8s component maps to something you already understand.
    By the end of Part 2, you'll deploy anihpj to a real K8s cluster.
 & Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%) — K8s is the deployment target
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: Without-vs-With K8s Comparison ➜ Shipping Port
     Analogy (Harbor Master→Docks→Ships→Cranes) ➜ K8s Architecture (Control
     Plane + Worker Nodes) ➜ API Server Request Flow ➜ etcd RAFT Consensus ➜
     Scheduler Filter→Score→Assign ➜ Controller Reconciliation Loop ➜ kubelet
     Node Agent ➜ kube-proxy Traffic Routing ➜ CRI Runtime Interface ➜ 9-Step
     Pod Creation Full Journey — 11 major architecture diagrams]

    4.1  Why Kubernetes? — The Problem It Solves
         - Running one container on one server is easy
         - Running 100 containers across 10 servers is chaos
         - What happens when a container crashes at 3 AM?
         - What happens when traffic spikes 10x?
         - Manual container management doesn't scale

         [WITHOUT K8s — Chaos]              [WITH K8s — Orchestrated]
         ┌────────────────────────┐         ┌────────────────────────┐
         │  💀 MANUAL MANAGEMENT   │         │  ☸️ AUTOMATED ORCHEST.  │
         │                        │         │                        │
         │  😰 "anihpj pod down!" │         │  ✅ Auto-restart in 2s  │
         │  😰 "Server 3 is full" │         │  ✅ Auto-schedule to    │
         │  😰 "Traffic 10x spike"│         │     free node           │
         │  😰 "Deploy at 2 AM"   │         │  ✅ HPA auto-scales up  │
         │  😰 "Rollback manually"│         │  ✅ Git push → deploy   │
         │  😰 "Which server has  │         │  ✅ kubectl rollout     │
         │      v2 deployed?"     │         │     undo (1 command)    │
         │  😰 "Update 100 config │         │  ✅ kubectl get pods    │
         │      files by hand"    │         │     -o wide (1 cmd)     │
         │                        │         │  ✅ kubectl apply -f    │
         │  YOU do everything.    │         │     (1 command)          │
         │  You are the SRE,      │         │  K8s IS your SRE team.  │
         │  the load balancer,    │         │  You DECLARE, it DOES.  │
         │  the restart script... │         │                         │
         └────────────────────────┘         └────────────────────────┘

    4.3  The Shipping Port Analogy — Understanding K8s Visually
         [🏗️ HARBOR MASTER ANALOGY — Every K8s Component as a Port Element]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🌊 KUBERNETES = A BUSY SHIPPING PORT                               │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │                 🏗️ PORT AUTHORITY (Control Plane)             │   │
         │  │                                                              │   │
         │  │  ┌─────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐   │   │
         │  │  │ 📡 SIGNAL    │  │ 📋 MANIFEST    │  │ 🎯 DISPATCH      │   │   │
         │  │  │ TOWER       │  │ OFFICE        │  │ TOWER            │   │   │
         │  │  │             │  │               │  │                  │   │   │
         │  │  │ kube-       │  │ etcd          │  │ kube-scheduler   │   │   │
         │  │  │ apiserver   │  │               │  │                  │   │   │
         │  │  │             │  │ "Container    │  │ "Ship anihpj to  │   │   │
         │  │  │ ALL ships   │  │  anihpj       │  │  Dock 3 — it     │   │   │
         │  │  │ must check  │  │  assigned     │  │  has enough      │   │   │
         │  │  │ in here!    │  │  to Dock 2"   │  │  crane capacity" │   │   │
         │  │  └─────────────┘  └───────────────┘  └──────────────────┘   │   │
         │  │                                                              │   │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │   │
         │  │  │  🔄 HARBOR MASTER OFFICE (kube-controller-manager)    │   │   │
         │  │  │                                                       │   │   │
         │  │  │  "Manifest says 3 anihpj ships. Dock 2 ship sank?     │   │   │
         │  │  │   Dispatch! Send replacement to Dock 5 NOW!"          │   │   │
         │  │  │                                                       │   │   │
         │  │  │  Constantly checks: Is actual = desired?              │   │   │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                    │                                  │
         │                                    │ "Send ships to docks!"           │
         │                                    ▼                                  │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │                     🏭 THE DOCKS (Worker Nodes)                │   │
         │  │                                                               │   │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────────┐    │   │
         │  │  │  DOCK 2 (Worker Node)   │  │  DOCK 5 (Worker Node)   │    │   │
         │  │  │                         │  │                         │    │   │
         │  │  │  ┌───────────────────┐  │  │  ┌───────────────────┐  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ 🏗️ CRANE          │  │  │  │ 🏗️ CRANE          │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ (kubelet)         │  │  │  │ (kubelet)         │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │                   │  │  │  │                   │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ Loads/unloads     │  │  │  │ "Dock 5 ready!    │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ containers from   │  │  │  │  Lifting anihpj   │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ ships. Reports    │  │  │  │  container onto   │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ to Signal Tower.  │  │  │  │  the ship..."     │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  └───────────────────┘  │  │  └───────────────────┘  │    │   │
         │  │  │                         │  │                         │    │   │
         │  │  │  ┌───────────────────┐  │  │  ┌───────────────────┐  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ 🚦 TRAFFIC CONTROL│  │  │  │ 🚦 TRAFFIC CONTROL│  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ (kube-proxy)      │  │  │  │ (kube-proxy)      │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │                   │  │  │  │                   │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ Directs trucks    │  │  │  │ "Truck for /jobs/ │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  │ to the right ship │  │  │  │  → Berth 3!"      │  │    │   │
         │  │  │  └───────────────────┘  │  │  └───────────────────┘  │    │   │
         │  │  │                         │  │                         │    │   │
         │  │  │  ═══════════════════   │  │  ═══════════════════   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ▓▓ SHIP (Pod) ▓▓▓▓   │  │  ▓▓ SHIP (Pod) ▓▓▓▓   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ▓ ┌───────────┐ ▓   │  │  ▓ ┌───────────┐ ▓   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ▓ │ 📦 anihpj │ ▓   │  │  ▓ │ 📦 anihpj │ ▓   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ▓ │ container │ ▓   │  │  ▓ │ container │ ▓   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ▓ │  :8000    │ ▓   │  │  ▓ │  :8000    │ ▓   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ▓ └───────────┘ ▓   │  │  ▓ └───────────┘ ▓   │    │   │
         │  │  │  ═══════════════════   │  │  ═══════════════════   │    │   │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────────┘    │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  MAPPING: Harbor Element → K8s Component                      │   │
         │  │                                                               │   │
         │  │  🏗️ Port Authority    = Control Plane (manages everything)    │   │
         │  │  📡 Signal Tower      = kube-apiserver (all comms here)       │   │
         │  │  📋 Manifest Office   = etcd (records where everything is)    │   │
         │  │  🎯 Dispatch Tower    = kube-scheduler (picks best dock)      │   │
         │  │  🔄 Harbor Master     = kube-controller-manager (maintains)   │   │
         │  │  🏭 Docks             = Worker Nodes (where ships dock)       │   │
         │  │  🏗️ Cranes            = kubelet (loads/unloads containers)    │   │
         │  │  🚦 Traffic Control   = kube-proxy (routes network traffic)   │   │
         │  │  🚢 Ships (Berths)    = Pods (run containers)                 │   │
         │  │  📦 Cargo Containers  = Docker Containers (your app)          │   │
         │  │  🚛 Trucks (cargo)    = Network Requests (user traffic)       │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.2  Kubernetes Defined — The Simple Explanation
         - Official: "Open-source container orchestration platform"
         - In plain English: K8s is the air traffic controller for your containers
         - You DECLARE what you want → K8s ENSURES it stays that way
         - "Desired State" vs "Actual State" — the core K8s concept
         - K8s = Kubernetes (K + 8 letters + s), Greek for "helmsman"
         [ANALOGY CARD: Kubernetes = Air Traffic Controller for containers]
         [ELEVATOR PITCH DIAGRAM: You declare → K8s delivers]

    4.3  The Shipping Port Analogy — Understanding K8s Visually
         - Harbor Master (Control Plane): coordinates everything
         - Ships (Pods): carry cargo (containers) to their destination
         - Docks (Nodes): where ships dock to load/unload
         - Cranes (kubelet): load containers onto ships
         - Shipping Manifest (etcd): records where everything is
         - Tugboats (Services): guide traffic to the right ship
         - Signal Tower (API Server): all communication goes through here
         [FULL HARBOR ANALOGY DIAGRAM: Animated shipping port visualization]
         [ANNOTATED: Each K8s component mapped to a harbor element]

    4.4  Kubernetes Architecture — Control Plane & Worker Nodes
         [COMPLETE ARCHITECTURE DIAGRAM]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                    KUBERNETES CLUSTER                       │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │           🧠 CONTROL PLANE (The Brain)                │  │
         │  │                                                       │  │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐ │  │
         │  │  │kube-     │  │ etcd │  │kube-     │  │kube-     │ │  │
         │  │  │apiserver │  │  💾  │  │scheduler │  │controller│ │  │
         │  │  │   🔌     │  │ Store│  │  🎯      │  │-manager  │ │  │
         │  │  │ All comm │  │All   │  │ Picks    │  │  🔄      │ │  │
         │  │  │via here  │  │State │  │ Node for │  │ Maintains│ │  │
         │  │  └──────────┘  └──────┘  │ Pod      │  │ Desired  │ │  │
         │  │                          └──────────┘  │ State    │ │  │
         │  │                                        └──────────┘ │  │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                          │                                  │
         │              "Desired State" vs "Actual State"              │
         │                          │                                  │
         │  ┌───────────────────────▼──────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │              💪 WORKER NODES (The Muscles)            │  │
         │  │                                                       │  │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐        │  │
         │  │  │   Worker Node 1  │    │   Worker Node 2  │        │  │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │  ┌────────────┐  │        │  │

         │  │  │  └────────────┘  │    │  └────────────┘  │        │  │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │  ┌────────────┐  │        │  │
         │  │  │  │ kube-proxy │  │    │  │ kube-proxy │  │        │  │
         │  │  │  └────────────┘  │    │  └────────────┘  │        │  │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │  ┌────────────┐  │        │  │
         │  │  │  │ Container  │  │    │  │ Container  │  │        │  │
         │  │  │  │ Runtime    │  │    │  │ Runtime    │  │        │  │
         │  │  │  │(containerd)│  │    │  │(containerd)│  │        │  │
         │  │  │  └────────────┘  │    │  └────────────┘  │        │  │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │  ┌────────────┐  │        │  │
         │  │  │  │ 📦 anihpj  │  │    │  │ 📦 anihpj  │  │        │  │
         │  │  │  │ Pod        │  │    │  │ Pod        │  │        │  │
         │  │  │  └────────────┘  │    │  └────────────┘  │        │  │

         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.5  Control Plane Components — Deep Dive
         4.5a kube-apiserver — The Front Door
              [API SERVER REQUEST FLOW]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  kubectl apply ──▶ API Server                             │
              │  ┌─────────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────────────────┐ │
              │  │ Authentication│─▶│Authorization│─▶│ Admission Control   │ │
              │  │ (Who are you?)│  │(Can you do │  │ (Mutating +         │ │
              │  │ cert/IAM/OIDC │  │  this?)    │  │  Validating         │ │
              │  │               │  │  RBAC      │  │  Webhooks)          │ │
              │  └──────────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────┬───────────────┘ │
              │                                         │ OK              │
              │                              ┌──────────▼───────────────┐ │
              │                              │ Write to etcd            │ │
              │                              │ Notify watchers          │ │
              │                              │ (scheduler, controllers) │ │
              │                              └──────────────────────────┘ │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         4.5b etcd — The Brain's Memory
              [etcd RAFT CONSENSUS — 3-Node HA Cluster]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  ┌─────────────────┐                                     │
              │  │  👑 LEADER       │  ← All writes go through leader    │
              │  │  (etcd-1)        │    Leader replicates to followers  │
              │  └────────┬────────┘                                     │
              │           │ replicate (RAFT log)                         │
              │  ┌────────▼────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐               │
              │  │  🙋 FOLLOWER     │  │  🙋 FOLLOWER     │               │
              │  │  (etcd-2)        │  │  (etcd-3)        │               │
              │  └─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘               │
              │                                                          │
              │  Quorum = (N/2)+1 = 2 (for 3-node cluster)              │
              │  💡 "If you lose etcd, you lose the cluster"             │
              │  Backup: etcdctl snapshot save → S3 cross-region         │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         4.5c kube-scheduler — The Matchmaker
              [SCHEDULER — Filter → Score → Assign]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  New Pod (Pending)                                        │
              │       │                                                   │
              │       ▼                                                   │
              │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
              │  │  FILTERING (Remove unfit nodes)                     │ │
              │  │  • Node has enough CPU? (request: 256m, node: 200m) │ │
              │  │  • Node has enough Memory? (request: 512Mi, free:1G)│ │
              │  │  • NodeSelector matches? (disk=ssd)                 │ │
              │  │  • Taints/Tolerations OK? (NoSchedule?)             │ │
              │  │  • Node is Ready? (SchedulingDisabled?)             │ │
              │  └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘ │
              │                         │ 3 nodes pass filter             │
              │                         ▼                                 │
              │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
              │  │  SCORING (Rank best fit)                            │ │
              │  │  • Node 3: Least requested (more free capacity) = 9 │ │
              │  │  • Node 5: Image already cached (anihpj:v1) = 8     │ │
              │  │  • Node 1: Pod affinity spread = 7                  │ │
              │  └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘ │
              │                         │ Node 3 wins!                   │
              │                         ▼                                 │
              │  Pod bound to Node 3 (pod.spec.nodeName = "node-3")      │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         4.5d kube-controller-manager — The Maintainer
              [CONTROLLER RECONCILIATION LOOP — Continuous Cycle]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │        ┌──────────────────────────┐                      │
              │        │   🔄 RECONCILIATION LOOP  │                      │
              │        │                          │                      │
              │  ┌─────▼──────┐    ┌──────────────┴──────┐              │
              │  │  OBSERVE   │    │       DIFF           │              │
              │  │            │───▶│                       │              │
              │  │ What IS    │    │ Desired (etcd)        │              │
              │  │ the current│    │ vs                    │              │
              │  │ state?     │    │ Actual (cluster)      │              │
              │  │            │    │                       │              │
              │  └─────▲──────┘    └──────────┬───────────┘              │
              │        │                      │                           │
              │        │         ┌────────────▼───────────┐              │
              │        └─────────│       ACT               │              │
              │                  │                         │              │
              │                  │ Apply changes to make   │              │
              │                  │ Actual = Desired        │              │
              │                  │ (create Pod, restart...)│              │
              │                  └─────────────────────────┘              │
              │                                                          │
              │  Example: anihpj Deployment wants 3 Pods, only 2 running  │
              │  → Controller creates 3rd Pod → Actual = Desired ✅       │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.6  Worker Node Components — Deep Dive
         4.6a kubelet — The Node Agent
              [KUBELET — Bridge Between API Server & Container Runtime]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  API Server                                              │
              │      │ Pod spec assigned to this node                     │
              │      ▼                                                    │
              │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
              │  │  kubelet (on Node-3)                              │    │
              │  │                                                   │    │
              │  │  1. Watch API server for Pods assigned to me      │    │
              │  │  2. Call CRI (containerd): Pull image if needed   │    │
              │  │  3. Create & start container                      │    │
              │  │  4. Run probes (liveness / readiness / startup)   │    │
              │  │  5. Report Pod status back to API server          │    │
              │  │  6. Monitor & restart if needed                   │    │
              │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         4.6b kube-proxy — The Network Proxy
              [KUBE-PROXY — Service → Pod Traffic Routing]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  User → anihpj Service (ClusterIP 10.100.50.2:8000)       │
              │                          │                                 │
              │                          ▼                                 │
              │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
              │  │  kube-proxy (iptables/IPVS rules on EVERY node)     │ │
              │  │                                                      │ │
              │  │  Service IP:10.100.50.2:8000 → Random backend:      │ │
              │  │    • Pod-1 (10.0.1.5:8000) — Node 1 — Healthy ✅   │ │
              │  │    • Pod-2 (10.0.2.8:8000) — Node 2 — Healthy ✅   │ │
              │  │    • Pod-3 (10.0.3.12:8000)— Node 3 — Healthy ✅   │ │
              │  │                                                      │ │
              │  │  Traffic is randomly distributed (default)           │ │
              │  │  or session-affinity (ClientIP)                      │ │
              │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         4.6c Container Runtime — The Engine
              [CRI — Container Runtime Interface]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  kubelet                                                  │
              │      │ CRI (Container Runtime Interface) gRPC             │
              │      ▼                                                    │
              │  ┌─────────────┐    ┌─────────────┐                      │
              │  │ containerd  │ OR │   CRI-O     │  ← K8s v1.24+        │
              │  │ (default)   │    │ (lightweight)│                      │
              │  └──────┬──────┘    └──────┬──────┘                      │
              │         │                  │                               │
              │         ▼                  ▼                               │
              │  ┌─────────────┐    ┌─────────────┐                      │
              │  │    runc     │    │    runc     │  ← OCI runtime       │
              │  │  (low-level)│    │  (low-level)│                      │
              │  └──────┬──────┘    └──────┬──────┘                      │
              │         │                  │                               │
              │         ▼                  ▼                               │
              │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
              │  │  📦 Container (anihpj) runs as Linux process     │     │
              │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
              │                                                          │
              │  ❌ dockershim removed in K8s v1.24 (Docker → containerd) │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
              - CRI (Container Runtime Interface) standard
              - Docker deprecation explained
              [DIAGRAM: CRI interface → containerd/CRI-O → runc → container]

    4.7  How a Pod Gets Created — The Full Journey
         [9-STEP POD CREATION JOURNEY — From kubectl to Running Container]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🚀 THE FULL JOURNEY: kubectl apply → Running Container (~2 sec)   │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 1: 👨‍💻 You run:                                            │  │
         │  │   kubectl apply -f deployment.yaml                            │  │
         │  │   "I want 3 anihpj Pods with image anihpj:v1"                 │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ HTTPS request                        │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 2: 📡 kube-apiserver                                     │  │
         │  │   • Authenticates you (kubeconfig cert / IAM / OIDC)           │  │
         │  │   • Authorizes you (RBAC: can you create Deployments?)        │  │
         │  │   • Validates the YAML (is apiVersion correct? kind valid?)   │  │
         │  │   • If OK → accepts request                                    │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ write                                │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 3: 💾 etcd (The Cluster Memory)                          │  │
         │  │   • Stores: "Deployment anihpj, desired: 3 replicas"           │  │
         │  │   • This is now the DESIRED STATE                             │  │
         │  │   • RAFT consensus: written to majority of etcd nodes         │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ watch event                          │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 4: 🔄 Deployment Controller (in kube-controller-manager) │  │
         │  │   • "New Deployment! I need to create a ReplicaSet..."        │  │
         │  │   • Creates ReplicaSet object → writes back to API server     │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ watch event                          │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 5: 🔄 ReplicaSet Controller                              │  │
         │  │   • "ReplicaSet anihpj-7d8f9 × 3 replicas needed"             │  │
         │  │   • "Currently 0 Pods → create 3 Pod objects"                 │  │
         │  │   • Creates 3 Pods (status: Pending, no node assigned yet)    │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ watch event                          │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 6: 🎯 kube-scheduler                                     │  │
         │  │   • "3 Pods need a home! Which nodes have space?"             │  │
         │  │   │ FILTER: Remove nodes that can't fit:                       │  │
         │  │   │  • Node 1: tainted (NoSchedule) → REMOVED                 │  │
         │  │   │  • Node 2: only 128Mi free (needs 256Mi) → REMOVED        │  │
         │  │   │  • Node 3: 4Gi free, no taints → CANDIDATE ✅            │  │
         │  │   │ SCORE: Rank candidates by fit                             │  │
         │  │   │  • Node 3: score 9.2/10 → ASSIGNED!                      │  │
         │  │   • Binds Pod anihpj-7d8f9-x1 to Node 3                       │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ pod.spec.nodeName = "node-3"         │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 7: 🏗️ kubelet (on Node 3)                                │  │
         │  │   • "New Pod assigned to me! Let me start it..."              │  │
         │  │   │ 1. Call CRI (containerd): "Pull image anihpj:v1"          │  │
         │  │   │    → containerd pulls from ECR (or local cache)           │  │
         │  │   │ 2. Create container from image                            │  │
         │  │   │ 3. Start container (runs gunicorn, port 8000)             │  │
         │  │   │ 4. Run liveness/readiness probes if defined               │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │ report status                        │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 8: 📡 kubelet → API server → etcd                        │  │
         │  │   • "Pod anihpj-7d8f9-x1 is now RUNNING on Node 3!"          │  │
         │  │   • ACTUAL STATE now matches DESIRED STATE for this Pod      │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                              │                                      │
         │                              ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ STEP 9: 🚦 kube-proxy (on ALL nodes)                          │  │
         │  │   • "New Pod with IP 10.0.3.45 on port 8000! Updating rules" │  │
         │  │   • Updates iptables/IPVS: traffic to Service → this Pod     │  │
         │  │   • Now users hitting the ALB → Service → Pod get response!  │  │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ⏱️ TOTAL TIME: ~2 seconds (image cached) to ~60 sec (large pull)   │
         │  🔁 Steps 6-9 repeat for EACH of the 3 Pod replicas!               │
         │  🔍 Run: kubectl get events --watch to see this live!              │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️  Chapter 4 Practice Drill: Explore a K8s Cluster
    📝  Chapter 4 Practice Questions (10 Questions)


Chapter 5: Core Kubernetes Objects — Pods, Deployments, Services     🟢 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%)
    🟢 BEGINNER NOTE: You only need to understand 5 K8s objects to be productive:
    Pod (runs your container), Deployment (manages Pods), Service (network
    endpoint), ConfigMap (settings), Secret (passwords). Everything else
    builds on these 5. Master these first, then explore the rest.
 — K8s objects are the deployment API
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~4 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: YAML 4 Required Fields ➜ Pod Anatomy (Shared
     Resources + Containers) ➜ ReplicaSet Self-Healing ➜ Deployment Hierarchy
     (Deploy→RS→Pod) ➜ Service Types Traffic Flow ➜ Namespace Boundaries ➜
     Labels & Selectors Glue ➜ ConfigMap/Secret Mount Patterns — 8 visuals]

    5.1  Kubernetes Objects — What Are They?
         [YAML OBJECT STRUCTURE — The 4 Required Fields]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: apps/v1          ← API Group/Version              │
         │  kind: Deployment             ← Object Type                    │
         │  metadata:                    ← Name, Labels, Namespace        │
         │    name: anihpj                                               │
         │    labels:                                                     │
         │      app: anihpj                                              │
         │  spec:                        ← DESIRED STATE (what you want)  │
         │    replicas: 3                                                 │
         │    selector:                                                   │
         │      matchLabels:                                              │
         │        app: anihpj                                            │
         │    template: ...                                               │
         │  status:                      ← ACTUAL STATE (set by K8s)     │
         │    availableReplicas: 2                                       │
         │    conditions: [...]                                           │
         │                                                                │
         │  💡 YOU write spec. K8s maintains status. The controller       │
         │     reconciliation loop drives spec → status!                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.2  Pods — The Smallest Deployable Unit
         5.2a What is a Pod?
              [POD ANATOMY — One Pod, One or More Containers]
              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
              │  📦 Pod: anihpj-7d8f9-x1  (IP: 10.0.3.45)               │
              │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
              │  │  Shared Resources (all containers share these):     │  │
              │  │  • Network namespace (localhost works!)            │  │
              │  │  • IPC namespace (shared memory)                   │  │
              │  │  • Volumes (same filesystem)                       │  │
              │  │  • Pod-level cgroups (CPU/Memory limits)           │  │
              │  ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤  │
              │  │  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐         │  │
              │  │  │ Container 1     │  │ Container 2     │         │  │
              │  │  │ anihpj (Django) │  │ nginx (proxy)   │         │  │
              │  │  │ Port: 8000      │  │ Port: 80        │         │  │
              │  │  └─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘         │  │
              │  │  Containers talk via localhost:8000 ←→ localhost:80│  │
              │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
              │  Pod Lifecycle: Pending → Running → Succeeded/Failed     │
              │  Restart: Always (default) | OnFailure | Never            │
              └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         5.2c Pod YAML for anihpj
              [CODE: Complete Pod YAML]
              apiVersion: v1
              kind: Pod
              metadata:
                name: anihpj-pod
                labels:
                  app: anihpj
              spec:
                containers:
                - name: anihpj
                  image: 123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1
                  ports:
                  - containerPort: 8000
                  env:
                  - name: DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE
                    value: anihpj.settings
                  resources:
                    requests: {memory: "256Mi", cpu: "250m"}
                    limits:   {memory: "512Mi", cpu: "500m"}
              [IMPERATIVE SHORTCUT]
              kubectl run anihpj --image=ECR_URI:v1 --dry-run=client -o yaml > pod.yaml

    5.3  ReplicaSets — Ensuring Pod Availability
         [REPLICASET — Maintains Desired Pod Count]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ReplicaSet: anihpj-7d8f9  (desired: 3)                      │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐                   │
         │  │ Pod-1 ✅ │  │ Pod-2 ✅ │  │ Pod-3 ✅ │  ← All running     │
         │  │ Node-1   │  │ Node-2   │  │ Node-3   │                   │
         │  └──────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────────┘                   │
         │                                                               │
         │  Pod-2 dies 💀 → ReplicaSet controller: "Only 2 running,     │
         │  need 3! Create new Pod-4 on Node-2!"                        │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐                   │
         │  │ Pod-1 ✅ │  │ Pod-4 ✅ │  │ Pod-3 ✅ │  ← Back to 3!     │
         │  └──────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────────┘                   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.4  Deployments — Managing Application Updates
         [DEPLOYMENT HIERARCHY — Deployment → ReplicaSet → Pod]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Deployment: anihpj (desired: 3 replicas, strategy: Rolling)  │
         │  │                                                            │
         │  ├── ReplicaSet: anihpj-v1-abc123  (revision 1, scaled to 0) │
         │  │   └── Pod × 0 (old version, no longer active)             │
         │  │                                                            │
         │  └── ReplicaSet: anihpj-v2-def456  (revision 2, active)       │
         │      ├── Pod-1 (anihpj:v2, Node-1) ✅                        │
         │      ├── Pod-2 (anihpj:v2, Node-2) ✅                        │
         │      └── Pod-3 (anihpj:v2, Node-3) ✅                        │
         │                                                                │
         │  ROLLING UPDATE: kubectl set image deploy/anihpj anihpj=v2   │
         │  → Creates new RS, gradually replaces Pods, zero downtime!   │
         │  ROLLBACK: kubectl rollout undo deploy/anihpj                │
         │  → Scales old RS back up, new RS down — instant!             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.5  Services — Exposing Your Application
         5.5a Why Services Exist
              - Pods die and are replaced with NEW IPs
              - Services provide a stable IP/DNS name
              - Service discovers Pods via label selectors
              [DIAGRAM: Service stable IP vs ephemeral Pod IPs]
         5.5b Service Types
              - ClusterIP (default): Internal only, within cluster
              - NodePort: Exposes on each Node's IP at a static port (30000-32767)
              - LoadBalancer: Creates AWS NLB/ALB (EKS integration)
              - ExternalName: DNS CNAME mapping
              [COMPARISON TABLE: Service types with use cases]
              [DIAGRAM: Each Service type traffic flow]
         5.5c Service YAML for anihpj
              [CODE: Service YAML — ClusterIP + LoadBalancer variants]
         5.5d Ingress — Advanced Routing
              - Path-based routing (/api → backend-service, / → frontend)
              - TLS termination, host-based routing
              - AWS Load Balancer Controller → ALB Ingress
              [CODE: Ingress YAML for anihpj with ALB annotations]

    5.6  Namespaces — Organizing Resources
         - Logical isolation within a cluster
         - Default namespaces: default, kube-system, kube-public, kube-node-lease
         - Resource quotas per namespace
         - Cross-namespace communication: <service>.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local
         [DIAGRAM: Namespace boundaries — dev/staging/prod isolation]
         [EXAMPLE: anihpj-dev, anihpj-staging, anihpj-prod namespaces]

    5.7  Labels, Selectors & Annotations — The Glue
         - Labels: key-value pairs for organizing/selecting objects
         - Selectors: filter objects by labels (equality-based, set-based)
         - Annotations: non-identifying metadata for tools/humans
         - Common label patterns: app, version, environment, tier
         [TABLE: Recommended labels (app.kubernetes.io/*)]
         [CODE: Label examples and kubectl selector usage]

    5.8  ConfigMaps & Secrets — Configuration Management
         - ConfigMaps: non-sensitive configuration (env vars, config files)
         - Secrets: sensitive data (passwords, tokens, API keys)
         - Mount as environment variables or volumes
         - AWS integration: External Secrets Operator + AWS Secrets Manager
         [CODE: ConfigMap YAML for anihpj Django settings]
         [CODE: Secret YAML for anihpj database credentials]
         [DIAGRAM: External Secrets Operator syncing AWS Secrets Manager → K8s Secrets]

Chapter 6: kubectl — Your Kubernetes Command Center                     🟢 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%) — kubectl is your primary tool
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~2 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: kubectl→kubeconfig→API Server Flow ➜ Imperative vs
     Declarative Decision Tree ➜ Command Categories Reference ➜ JSONPath Output
     Formatting Examples — 4 visual references]


         [KUBECTL → KUBECONFIG → API SERVER FLOW]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🖥️  YOUR TERMINAL                  ☸️  KUBERNETES CLUSTER     │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐ │
         │  │   kubectl    │────▶│  kubeconfig   │────▶│ kube-        │ │
         │  │   command    │     │  (~/.kube/    │     │ apiserver    │ │
         │  │              │     │   config)     │     │              │ │
         │  │ kubectl get  │     │               │     │ Authenticate │ │
         │  │ pods         │     │ • cluster URL │     │ + Authorize  │ │
         │  │              │     │ • certificate │     │ + Validate   │ │
         │  │ kubectl      │     │ • user creds  │     │              │ │
         │  │ apply -f     │     │ • namespace   │     │ Execute      │ │
         │  │ deploy.yaml  │     │ • context     │     │ request      │ │
         │  └──────────────┘     └──────────────┘     └──────┬───────┘ │
         │                                                    │         │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
         │  │                                                           │
         │  ▼                                                           │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │              KUBERNETES CLUSTER                      │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  │    │
         │  │  │   etcd   │  │scheduler │  │controller-manager│  │    │
         │  │  │ (store)  │  │ (place)  │  │  (maintain)      │  │    │
         │  │  └──────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────────────────┘  │    │
         │  │                                                      │    │
         │  │  ┌────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐            │    │
         │  │  │  Worker Node 1 │  │  Worker Node 2 │  ...       │    │
         │  │  │  ┌──────────┐  │  │  ┌──────────┐  │            │    │
         │  │  │  │ anihpj 📦 │  │  │  │ anihpj 📦 │  │            │    │
         │  │  │  └──────────┘  │  │  └──────────┘  │            │    │
         │  │  └────────────────┘  └────────────────┘            │    │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    6.1  kubectl Basics — The Universal K8s Tool
         - kubectl is your CLI for everything K8s
         - Syntax: kubectl <verb> <resource> [flags]
         - Connects to cluster via kubeconfig file (~/.kube/config)
         - Multiple contexts for multiple clusters
         [DIAGRAM: kubectl → kubeconfig → API server → cluster]
         [CODE: kubeconfig file structure explained]


         [IMPERATIVE vs DECLARATIVE — DECISION TREE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🤔 SHOULD I USE IMPERATIVE OR DECLARATIVE?                   │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────────┐        │
         │  │  🏃 IMPERATIVE        │    │  📝 DECLARATIVE       │        │
         │  │  "Do this NOW"       │    │  "Make it LIKE this" │        │
         │  ├──────────────────────┤    ├──────────────────────┤        │
         │  │                      │    │                      │        │
         │  │ kubectl run          │    │ kubectl apply -f     │        │
         │  │ kubectl create       │    │  deployment.yaml     │        │
         │  │ kubectl expose       │    │                      │        │
         │  │ kubectl scale        │    │ ✅ Version controlled │        │
         │  │ kubectl set image    │    │ ✅ Repeatable         │        │
         │  │                      │    │ ✅ Reviewable (PR)    │        │
         │  │ ✅ Fast (exam!)      │    │ ✅ Auditable          │        │
         │  │ ✅ Simple            │    │ ✅ Self-documenting   │        │
         │  │ ✅ One-off tasks     │    │ ✅ GitOps compatible  │        │
         │  │                      │    │                      │        │
         │  │ USE FOR:             │    │ USE FOR:             │        │
         │  │ • Exam speed         │    │ • Production deploys │        │
         │  │ • Debugging          │    │ • CI/CD pipelines    │        │
         │  │ • Quick tests        │    │ • Team environments  │        │
         │  │ • Dry-run YAML gen   │    │ • GitOps workflows   │        │
         │  └──────────────────────┘    └──────────────────────┘        │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 EXAM TIP: Use imperative for YAML generation,             │
         │     then apply declaratively for consistency!                 │
         │     k run pod --image=nginx --dry-run=client -o yaml > p.yaml│
         │     k apply -f p.yaml                                        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    6.2  Imperative vs Declarative Commands
         - Imperative: "Do this now" (kubectl run, kubectl expose, kubectl scale)
         - Declarative: "Make it look like this" (kubectl apply -f manifest.yaml)
         - When to use each (exam speed tip: imperative is faster!)
         [COMPARISON TABLE: Imperative vs Declarative with examples]

    6.3  Essential kubectl Commands Reference
         [COMPREHENSIVE COMMAND TABLES — 6 categories]
         6.3a Viewing / Inspecting Commands
              kubectl get pods/nodes/services/deployments -o wide
              kubectl describe pod <name>
              kubectl logs -f <pod> -c <container>
              kubectl exec -it <pod> -- bash
         6.3b Creating / Applying Commands
              kubectl apply -f manifest.yaml
              kubectl create deployment anihpj --image=anihpj:v1
              kubectl run temp --image=busybox --rm -it -- sh
         6.3c Modifying Commands
              kubectl edit deployment anihpj
              kubectl scale deployment anihpj --replicas=5
              kubectl set image deployment/anihpj anihpj=anihpj:v2
              kubectl rollout undo deployment/anihpj
         6.3d Deleting Commands
              kubectl delete pod <name>
              kubectl delete -f manifest.yaml
              kubectl delete deployment,service -l app=anihpj
         6.3e Debugging Commands
              kubectl describe pod <name>
              kubectl get events --sort-by=.metadata.creationTimestamp
              kubectl top pods/nodes
              kubectl port-forward pod/anihpj 8080:8000
         6.3f Cluster Admin Commands
              kubectl get nodes -o wide
              kubectl cordon/uncordon/drain <node>
              kubectl taint nodes <node> key=value:NoSchedule

    6.4  kubectl Output Formats & Filtering
         - -o wide, -o yaml, -o json
         - JSONPath: kubectl get pods -o jsonpath='{.items[*].status.podIP}'
         - --sort-by, --selector (-l), --field-selector
         - Custom columns: -o custom-columns=NAME:.metadata.name,IP:.status.podIP
         [CODE EXAMPLES: Common JSONPath expressions for the exam]


         [KUBECTL RESOURCE SHORTCUTS — VISUAL CHEAT SHEET]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🏎️  KUBECTL SPEED TIPS — EVERY ABBREVIATION YOU NEED         │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┐              │
         │  │  FULL NAME    │  SHORTCUT    │  EXAMPLE      │              │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┤              │
         │  │  pods         │  po          │ k get po     │              │
         │  │  services     │  svc         │ k get svc    │              │
         │  │  deployments  │  deploy      │ k get deploy │              │
         │  │  replicasets  │  rs          │ k get rs     │              │
         │  │  namespaces   │  ns          │ k get ns     │              │
         │  │  nodes        │  no          │ k get no     │              │
         │  │  configmaps   │  cm          │ k get cm     │              │
         │  │  secrets      │  (none)      │ k get secret │              │
         │  │  persistentvol│  pv          │ k get pv     │              │
         │  │  pvc          │  pvc         │ k get pvc    │              │
         │  │  ingresses    │  ing         │ k get ing    │              │
         │  │  statefulsets │  sts         │ k get sts    │              │
         │  │  daemonsets   │  ds          │ k get ds     │              │
         │  │  jobs         │  (none)      │ k get job    │              │
         │  │  cronjobs     │  cj          │ k get cj     │              │
         │  │  serviceaccts │  sa          │ k get sa     │              │
         │  │  clusterroles │  (none)      │ k get clusterrole│       │
         │  └──────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────┘              │
         │                                                               │
         │  ⏱️  DRY-RUN YAML GENERATION (memorize this pattern!):        │
         │  export do='--dry-run=client -o yaml'                        │
         │  k create deploy web --image=nginx $do > deploy.yaml         │
         │  k run test --image=busybox $do > pod.yaml                   │
         │  k create cm settings --from-literal=DEBUG=false $do          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    6.5  kubectl Shortcuts & Time-Savers
         - Resource aliases: po (pods), svc (services), deploy (deployments), ns (namespaces)
         - Dry-run YAML generation: --dry-run=client -o yaml
         - Quick pod creation: kubectl run test --image=nginx --dry-run=client -o yaml > pod.yaml
         - Force delete: kubectl delete pod x --force --grace-period=0
         [TABLE: All common resource name abbreviations]

Chapter 7: YAML Configuration Files — The Language of Kubernetes     🟢 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%)
    🟢 BEGINNER NOTE: YAML looks scary at first — it's JUST key-value pairs
    with indentation (like Python!). No curly braces, no semicolons. The
    "4 required fields" (apiVersion, kind, metadata, spec) appear in
    EVERY K8s YAML file. Once you memorize those 4, you can write ANY
    Kubernetes manifest. PROMISE: It's easier than it looks!
 — YAML is the IaC for K8s
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~2 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: YAML Syntax Rules ➜ 4 Required Fields Annotated
     ➜ apiVersion Quick Reference Table ➜ Complete anihpj YAML Template Library
     ➜ kubectl explain Tree ➜ Multi-Document YAML (--- separator) — 6 visuals]


         [YAML vs JSON — SIDE-BY-SIDE COMPARISON]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  📝 YAML (Kubernetes Standard)     📊 JSON (Alternative)      │
         │  ─────────────────────────────     ─────────────────────      │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ apiVersion: v1       │         │ {                    │   │
         │  │ kind: Pod            │         │   "apiVersion": "v1",│   │
         │  │ metadata:            │         │   "kind": "Pod",     │   │
         │  │   name: anihpj       │         │   "metadata": {      │   │
         │  │   labels:            │         │     "name": "anihpj",│   │
         │  │     app: anihpj      │         │     "labels": {      │   │
         │  │ spec:                │         │       "app": "anihpj"│   │
         │  │   containers:        │         │     }                │   │
         │  │   - name: web        │         │   },                 │   │
         │  │     image: nginx     │         │   "spec": {...}      │   │
         │  └──────────────────────┘         │ }                    │   │
         │                                    └──────────────────────┘   │
         │  ✅ Human-readable              ❌ Noisy braces/commas        │
         │  ✅ No closing tags             ❌ Forgetting } breaks JSON   │
         │  ✅ Comments with #             ❌ No comments in JSON!        │
         │  ✅ Multi-doc with ---          ❌ One document per file       │
         │  ✅ K8s native format           ⚠️  K8s accepts both           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    7.1  Why YAML? — The Configuration Language
         - Human-readable, whitespace-sensitive (2-space indentation)
         - Maps (key: value), Lists (- item), Nested structures
         - YAML vs JSON: YAML is the K8s standard
         [YAML CRASH COURSE: Key syntax rules with examples]
         [TABLE: YAML data types — strings, numbers, booleans, null, lists, maps]


         [YAML 4 REQUIRED FIELDS — ANNOTATED DIAGRAM]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  📋 EVERY KUBERNETES YAML HAS THESE 4 FIELDS                  │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  apiVersion: apps/v1          ◀── WHICH API version?   │    │
         │  │  kind: Deployment             ◀── WHAT are you creating?│    │
         │  │  metadata:                    ◀── WHAT is it called?    │    │
         │  │    name: anihpj-deployment   │  (name, labels, ns)     │    │
         │  │    labels:                    │                          │    │
         │  │      app: anihpj             │                          │    │
         │  │  spec:                        ◀── WHAT should it do?    │    │
         │  │    replicas: 3               │  (DESIRED state: you    │    │
         │  │    selector:                 │   declare, K8s ensures) │    │
         │  │      matchLabels:            │                          │    │
         │  │        app: anihpj           │                          │    │
         │  │    template: ...             │                          │    │
         │  │                              │                          │    │
         │  │  status:                     ◀── WHAT is happening?     │    │
         │  │    availableReplicas: 2     │  (ACTUAL state: K8s     │    │
         │  │    conditions: [...]        │   reports this back)    │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 YOU write apiVersion + kind + metadata + spec.            │
         │     K8s writes status. Controllers reconcile spec → status.  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    7.2  The Four Required Fields in Every K8s YAML
         - apiVersion: Which API group/version (apps/v1, v1, networking.k8s.io/v1)
         - kind: Object type (Pod, Deployment, Service, ConfigMap)
         - metadata: name, namespace, labels, annotations
         - spec: The desired state (varies by kind)
         [ANNOTATED YAML DIAGRAM: Each field explained with visual connections]

    7.3  Common apiVersion Quick Reference
         [TABLE: Resource → apiVersion mappings]
         | Resource          | apiVersion                        |
         | Pod               | v1                                |
         | Service           | v1                                |
         | ConfigMap         | v1                                |
         | Secret            | v1                                |
         | Deployment        | apps/v1                           |
         | ReplicaSet        | apps/v1                           |
         | StatefulSet       | apps/v1                           |
         | DaemonSet         | apps/v1                           |
         | Job/CronJob       | batch/v1                          |
         | Ingress           | networking.k8s.io/v1              |
         | NetworkPolicy     | networking.k8s.io/v1              |
         | HPA               | autoscaling/v2                    |
         | PV/PVC            | v1                                |
         | StorageClass      | storage.k8s.io/v1                 |

         [kubectl explain — VISUAL HIERARCHY TREE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🌲 kubectl explain deployment.spec — FIELD HIERARCHY         │
         │                                                               │
         │  Deployment                                                   │
         │  └── spec                                                     │
         │      ├── replicas (integer) ← how many copies?               │
         │      ├── selector (LabelSelector) ← which Pods to manage?    │
         │      │   ├── matchLabels (map[string]string)                 │
         │      │   └── matchExpressions ([]LabelSelectorRequirement)   │
         │      ├── strategy (DeploymentStrategy) ← how to update?      │
         │      │   ├── type: RollingUpdate | Recreate                   │
         │      │   └── rollingUpdate:                                   │
         │      │       ├── maxSurge: 25%                                │
         │      │       └── maxUnavailable: 25%                          │
         │      ├── template (PodTemplateSpec) ← Pod blueprint          │
         │      │   ├── metadata (ObjectMeta)                            │
         │      │   └── spec (PodSpec)                                   │
         │      │       ├── containers ([]Container)                     │
         │      │       │   ├── name (string)                            │
         │      │       │   ├── image (string)                           │
         │      │       │   ├── ports ([]ContainerPort)                  │
         │      │       │   ├── resources (ResourceRequirements)         │
         │      │       │   │   ├── requests (ResourceList)              │
         │      │       │   │   └── limits (ResourceList)                │
         │      │       │   └── env ([]EnvVar)                           │
         │      │       ├── volumes ([]Volume)                           │
         │      │       └── serviceAccountName (string)                  │
         │      └── revisionHistoryLimit (integer)                       │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 Run: kubectl explain deployment.spec --recursive          │
         │     to see the FULL tree! Your in-exam documentation!         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 8: AWS Compute Services — Where Your Code Runs              🟢 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%) & Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%)
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: EC2+ASG Architecture ➜ ECS vs EKS Decision Matrix
     ➜ Lambda Event Sources Flow ➜ Fargate vs Lambda vs EC2 Comparison ➜
     Compute Decision Tree (Which service for anihpj?) ➜ EC2 Deployment — 6 visuals]

    8.1  EC2 (Elastic Compute Cloud)
         - Instance types, AMIs, launch templates
         - Auto Scaling Groups — the DevOps essential
         - Placement groups, Hibernate, ENI
         - EC2 Image Builder for golden AMIs
         [DIAGRAM: EC2 + ASG architecture — scale out/in based on CloudWatch]

    8.2  ECS (Elastic Container Service)
         - AWS-native container orchestration
         - ECS vs EKS — when to use which
         - Fargate vs EC2 launch type
         - Task definitions, services, capacity providers
         [COMPARISON TABLE: ECS vs EKS decision matrix]
         [DIAGRAM: ECS architecture — task → service → cluster]

    8.3  Lambda — Serverless Compute
         - Event-driven, pay-per-invocation
         - Triggers: S3, API Gateway, EventBridge, DynamoDB Streams
         - Cold starts, concurrency, execution limits
         - Lambda + CodeDeploy for canary deployments
         [DIAGRAM: Lambda event sources and invocation flow]

    8.4  Fargate — Serverless Containers
         - No EC2 management, just run containers
         - Works with both ECS and EKS
         - Fargate pricing model
         [COMPARISON: Fargate vs Lambda vs EC2 for containers]


         [COMPUTE DECISION TREE — Which AWS Service for Your App?]

Chapter 9: AWS Networking — VPC, Subnets & Connectivity             🟢 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%) — Networking is the backbone
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: Complete VPC Architecture ➜ EKS VPC Layout ➜
     ALB vs NLB vs GWLB Comparison ➜ ALB→EKS Pods via LB Controller ➜ Route 53
     →ALB→EKS Traffic Flow ➜ CloudFront→S3+ALB for anihpj ➜ API Gateway→EKS — 7 visuals]

    9.1  VPC (Virtual Private Cloud) Fundamentals
         - CIDR blocks, subnets (public vs private)
         - Route tables, Internet Gateway, NAT Gateway
         - Security Groups (stateful) vs NACLs (stateless)
         - VPC Flow Logs for network monitoring
         [DIAGRAM: Complete VPC architecture with public/private subnets]

    9.2  VPC Design Patterns for EKS
         - Recommended: Public + Private subnets across 3 AZs
         - Control plane endpoint: Public, Private, Public+Private
         - NAT Gateway for private subnet outbound traffic
         - VPC CNI plugin — Pods get real VPC IPs
         [DIAGRAM: EKS VPC layout — subnets, endpoints, Pod networking]

    9.3  Load Balancing on AWS
         - ALB (Application Load Balancer): Layer 7, HTTP/HTTPS, path routing
         - NLB (Network Load Balancer): Layer 4, TCP/UDP, high throughput
         - GWLB (Gateway Load Balancer): Layer 3, virtual appliances
         - CLB (Classic Load Balancer): Legacy, avoid for new deployments
         [COMPARISON TABLE: ALB vs NLB — features, protocols, use cases]
         [DIAGRAM: ALB routing to EKS Pods via AWS Load Balancer Controller]

    9.4  Route 53 — DNS & Traffic Management
         - Hosted zones, record types (A, CNAME, Alias)
         - Routing policies: Simple, Weighted, Latency, Failover, Geolocation
         - Health checks and DNS failover
         - Route 53 + ALB + EKS = production anihpj
         [DIAGRAM: Route 53 → ALB → EKS Pods traffic flow]

    9.5  CloudFront — Content Delivery Network
         - Edge locations, origins (S3, ALB, API Gateway)
         - Cache behaviors, TTL, invalidation
         - OAC (Origin Access Control) for S3
         - Lambda@Edge and CloudFront Functions
         [DIAGRAM: CloudFront → S3 static + ALB dynamic for anihpj]

    9.6  API Gateway — Managed API Management
         - REST API vs HTTP API vs WebSocket API
         - Stages, deployment, canary releases
         - Integration with Lambda and EKS
         [DIAGRAM: API Gateway → Lambda/VPC Link → EKS backend]

Chapter 10: AWS IAM — Identity & Access Management                    🟢 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 6 (Security 17%) — IAM is the #1 security topic
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: IAM Entity Relationships ➜ Policy Evaluation Logic
     Flowchart ➜ EC2 Instance Role Flow ➜ IRSA Token Exchange (OIDC→IAM→Pod)
     ➜ AWS Organizations SCP Inheritance — 5 major security diagrams]

    10.1 IAM Fundamentals
         - Users, Groups, Roles (the holy trinity)
         - Policies: JSON documents defining permissions
         - Effect, Action, Resource, Condition
         - IAM is GLOBAL (not regional) — important exam fact!
         [DIAGRAM: IAM entity relationships — Users in Groups, assume Roles]

    10.2 IAM Policies — Deep Dive
         - Identity-based vs Resource-based policies
         - Permission boundaries, session policies
         - Policy evaluation logic (explicit DENY > ALLOW)
         - IAM Policy Simulator
         [FLOWCHART: IAM policy evaluation — DENY → SCP → Resource → Identity → Boundary]
         [CODE: IAM policy examples for DevOps workflows]

    10.3 IAM Roles — The DevOps Essential
         - EC2 Instance Roles (no more hardcoded credentials!)
         - Service Roles (Lambda, CodeBuild, CodePipeline)
         - Cross-account roles (prod access from ops account)
         - Web Identity Federation (Cognito, OIDC)
         [DIAGRAM: EC2 instance → IAM Role → S3 access — no access keys needed]


         [IRSA — IAM ROLES FOR SERVICE ACCOUNTS — TOKEN EXCHANGE FLOW]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔐 HOW IRSA WORKS — Pod Gets Temporary AWS Credentials       │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │  STEP 1: SETUP (one-time per cluster)                     ││
         │  │                                                           ││
         │  │  AWS IAM OIDC Provider ←── EKS Cluster (OIDC endpoint)   ││
         │  │  (Trusts EKS cluster to issue tokens)                     ││
         │  │                                                           ││
         │  │  IAM Role: anihpj-s3-read-role                            ││
         │  │  Trust Policy: "Allow EKS OIDC to assume this role        ││
         │  │                 IF service account = anihpj-sa"           ││
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
         │                          │                                    │
         │                          ▼                                    │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │  STEP 2: RUNTIME (every Pod start)                        ││
         │  │                                                           ││
         │  │  ┌────────────┐    ① Projected Token      ┌────────────┐ ││
         │  │  │  anihpj    │───────────────────────────▶│ AWS STS    │ ││
         │  │  │  Pod       │    "I am anihpj-sa         │            │ ││
         │  │  │            │     in namespace anihpj"   │ Verify JWT │ ││
         │  │  │ K8s Service│◀───────────────────────────│ signature  │ ││
         │  │  │ Account:   │    ② Temporary AWS Creds   │ against    │ ││
         │  │  │ anihpj-sa  │    (AccessKey, SecretKey,  │ OIDC       │ ││
         │  │  │            │     SessionToken)          │ provider   │ ││
         │  │  │ Annotation:│                             └────────────┘ ││
         │  │  │ eks.amazon │                                            ││
         │  │  │ aws.com/   │  ③ Use creds to call AWS API              ││
         │  │  │ role-arn   │──────────────────────────▶  ┌────────────┐ ││
         │  │  └────────────┘                              │ Amazon S3  │ ││
         │  │                                              │ (anihpj    │ ││
         │  │  RESULT: Pod can access S3 WITHOUT           │  bucket)   │ ││
         │  │  hardcoded credentials! ✅                   └────────────┘ ││
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 NO MORE HARDCODED AWS KEYS IN PODS!                       │
         │     Credentials auto-rotate. Least privilege per Pod.         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    10.4 IRSA — IAM Roles for Service Accounts (EKS)
         - Pods need AWS permissions (S3, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager)
         - The problem: Nodes have broad permissions, all Pods share them
         - The solution: IRSA — per-Pod IAM roles via OIDC
         - How IRSA works: OIDC provider → IAM Role → K8s Service Account → Pod
         [STEP-BY-STEP DIAGRAM: IRSA setup and token exchange flow]
         [CODE: IRSA setup — OIDC provider, IAM Role, Service Account annotation]

    10.5 AWS Organizations & SCPs
         - Multi-account strategy: Management, Security, Log Archive, Workloads
         - Organizational Units (OUs) for grouping
         - SCPs: Guardrails (what accounts CAN'T do, even with IAM Allow)
         - Tag policies, backup policies, AI opt-out policies
         [DIAGRAM: Organization structure with SCP inheritance]

    10.6 IAM Best Practices for DevOps
         - Principle of least privilege (start with nothing, add what's needed)
         - Use Roles over Users (no long-lived credentials)
         - Enable MFA for all human users
         - Rotate access keys regularly
         - Use IAM Access Analyzer
         - Monitor with IAM Credential Report

    🏋️  Chapter 10 Practice Drill: Set Up IRSA for anihpj
    📝  Chapter 10 Practice Questions (10 Questions)


Chapter 11: AWS Storage & Database Services                            🟢 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%) & Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%)
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: S3 Lifecycle Transitions ➜ EBS Volume Types Matrix
     ➜ EFS Multi-AZ Mount to EKS ➜ RDS Multi-AZ Architecture ➜ DynamoDB Global
     Tables ➜ ElastiCache Caching Layer for anihpj — 6 storage visuals]

    11.1 S3 (Simple Storage Service)
         - Object storage, unlimited scale, 11 9's durability
         - Storage classes: Standard, IA, Glacier, Deep Archive
         - Lifecycle policies, versioning, replication
         - S3 Event Notifications → Lambda, SQS, SNS
         [DIAGRAM: S3 lifecycle — object transitions between storage classes]
         [USE CASE: anihpj static files (CSS, JS, images) on S3 + CloudFront]

    11.2 EBS (Elastic Block Store)
         - Block storage for EC2 instances
         - Volume types: gp3, io2, st1, sc1
         - Snapshots and AMI creation
         - EBS vs Instance Store
         [TABLE: EBS volume types — price, performance, use cases]

    11.3 EFS (Elastic File System)
         - NFS-compatible file storage
         - Multi-AZ access, auto-scaling
         - EFS with EKS: ReadWriteMany volumes for shared storage
         [DIAGRAM: EFS mounted across multiple AZs to EKS nodes/Pods]

    11.4 RDS (Relational Database Service)
         - Managed databases: PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, Oracle, SQL Server
         - Multi-AZ deployment, Read Replicas
         - Automated backups, snapshots, point-in-time recovery
         - RDS Proxy for connection pooling
         [DIAGRAM: RDS Multi-AZ — Primary + Standby in different AZs]
         [CODE: anihpj connecting to RDS PostgreSQL from EKS]

    11.5 DynamoDB — NoSQL Database
         - Serverless, single-digit millisecond latency
         - On-demand vs Provisioned capacity
         - DynamoDB Streams + Lambda
         - Global Tables (multi-region)
         [USE CASE: jobpost application tracking with DynamoDB]

    11.6 ElastiCache — In-Memory Caching
         - Redis vs Memcached
         - Session management, query caching
         - Cluster mode for Redis
         [DIAGRAM: anihpj with ElastiCache caching layer]

    11.7 Database Migration Strategies
         - AWS DMS (Database Migration Service)
         - SCT (Schema Conversion Tool)
         - Blue/Green deployments for database updates

Chapter 12: CI/CD Fundamentals — Concepts & Strategies                🟡 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%) — CI/CD is THE core DevOps domain
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: CI/CD Pipeline Flow ➜ 6 Deployment Strategies
     (Rolling/Blue-Green/Canary/AB/Linear/All-at-Once) ➜ Complete anihpj CI/CD
     Pipeline on AWS (Source→Build→Test→Deploy→Approve→Prod) — 8 visuals]

    12.1 What is CI/CD?
         - Continuous Integration: merge code frequently, run automated tests
         - Continuous Delivery: every change is releasable
         - Continuous Deployment: every change goes to production automatically
         [CI/CD PIPELINE DIAGRAM: Code → Build → Test → Deploy → Monitor]

    12.2 Deployment Strategies — Complete Guide
         12.2a All-at-Once (In-Place)
               - Stop old, start new — downtime!
               - Fastest but riskiest
         12.2b Rolling Update
               - Replace instances one by one, zero downtime
               - Default in Kubernetes Deployments
               [DIAGRAM: Rolling update — Pod-by-Pod replacement]
         12.2c Blue/Green Deployment
               - Two identical environments, switch traffic at once
               - Instant rollback by switching back
               - Requires double capacity during deploy
               [DIAGRAM: Blue → Green traffic switch via Route 53/ALB]
         12.2d Canary Deployment
               - Send small % of traffic to new version
               - Gradually increase if metrics are healthy
               - Advanced: automated rollback if errors spike
               [DIAGRAM: Canary — 10% → 25% → 50% → 100% traffic shifting]
         12.2e A/B Testing
               - Route users based on cookies/headers
               - Compare metrics between variants
         12.2f Linear (Traffic Shifting)
               - CodeDeploy Lambda/ECS — increment traffic in steps
         [COMPARISON TABLE: All 6 deployment strategies — risk, speed, rollback ease]

    12.3 CI/CD Best Practices
         - Small, frequent commits (merge daily, not weekly)
         - Fast feedback loops (tests must complete in < 10 minutes)
         - Immutable infrastructure (never patch, always rebuild)
         - Everything as code (pipelines, infrastructure, config)
         - Shift left (security, testing, compliance as early as possible)

    12.4 CI/CD for anihpj — Pipeline Design
         [COMPLETE ANIHpj CI/CD PIPELINE — AWS Implementation]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔄 ANIHpj CI/CD PIPELINE ON AWS                                    │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ┌──────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌──────────┐        │
         │  │  SOURCE  │──▶│  BUILD   │──▶│   TEST   │──▶│  DEPLOY  │        │
         │  │          │   │          │   │          │   │          │        │
         │  │ GitHub/  │   │CodeBuild │   │Unit Tests│   │ EKS Dev  │        │
         │  │ CodeCmmit│   │          │   │Security  │   │ Namespace│        │
         │  │          │   │ 🐋 Docker │   │ Scan     │   │          │        │
         │  │ git push │   │  build   │   │          │   │ Rolling  │        │
         │  │ triggers │   │  push ECR│   │ CodeGuru │   │ Update   │        │
         │  │ pipeline │   │          │   │          │   │          │        │
         │  └──────────┘   └──────────┘   └──────────┘   └─────┬────┘        │
         │                                                      │             │
         │                                                      ▼             │
         │                                    ┌──────────────────────────┐   │
         │                                    │     EKS Staging           │   │
         │                                    │  Blue/Green Deployment    │   │
         │                                    │  Run integration tests    │   │
         │                                    └────────────┬─────────────┘   │
         │                                                 │                  │
         │                                                 ▼                  │
         │                                    ┌──────────────────────────┐   │
         │                                    │  👤 MANUAL APPROVAL       │   │
         │                                    │  (SNS → Slack/Email)      │   │
         │                                    └────────────┬─────────────┘   │
         │                                                 │                  │
         │                                                 ▼                  │
         │                                    ┌──────────────────────────┐   │
         │                                    │     EKS Production        │   │
         │                                    │  Canary 10% → 25% → 100% │   │
         │                                    │  CloudWatch Alarms watch  │   │
         │                                    │  Auto-rollback on error   │   │
         │                                    └──────────────────────────┘   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

     CodeBuild→ECR Push Flow ➜ CodeArtifact Upstream Repos — 4 visuals]

    13.1 CodeCommit — Git Repositories on AWS
         - Managed Git, encrypted at rest
         - IAM-based authentication (no SSH keys needed for HTTPS)
