         [CODEBUILD BUILDSPEC.YML — PHASE-BY-PHASE FLOW]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🏗️  buildspec.yml — THE BUILD RECIPE FOR anihpj               │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  version: 0.2                                        │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │ PHASE 1: install                                 │ │    │
         │  │  │ ─────────────────                                │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Install system packages (gcc, libpq-dev)       │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Install Python 3.12                            │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Install Docker (for building image)            │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Runtime: ~60 seconds                           │ │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                         │                             │    │
         │  │                         ▼                             │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │ PHASE 2: pre_build                               │ │    │
         │  │  │ ─────────────────────                             │ │    │
         │  │  │ • pip install -r requirements.txt                │ │    │
         │  │  │ • aws ecr get-login-password | docker login ECR │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Set IMAGE_TAG = $CODEBUILD_BUILD_NUMBER        │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Runtime: ~90 seconds                           │ │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                         │                             │    │
         │  │                         ▼                             │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │ PHASE 3: build                                   │ │    │
         │  │  │ ──────────────                                    │ │    │
         │  │  │ • docker build -t anihpj:$IMAGE_TAG .            │ │    │
         │  │  │ • docker tag anihpj:$IMAGE_TAG ECR_URI:latest   │ │    │
         │  │  │ • python manage.py collectstatic --noinput       │ │    │
         │  │  │ • python manage.py test                          │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Runtime: ~180 seconds                          │ │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                         │                             │    │
         │  │                         ▼                             │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │ PHASE 4: post_build                              │ │    │
         │  │  │ ────────────────────                              │ │    │
         │  │  │ • docker push ECR_URI:$IMAGE_TAG                 │ │    │
         │  │  │ • docker push ECR_URI:latest                     │ │    │
         │  │  │ • aws ecr start-image-scan (vulnerability check) │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Create imagedefinitions.json for CodeDeploy    │ │    │
         │  │  │ • Runtime: ~120 seconds                          │ │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  artifacts:                                           │    │
         │  │    files: imagedefinitions.json                       │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  ⏱️  TOTAL BUILD TIME: ~7 minutes                              │
         │  📦  OUTPUT: Docker image in ECR + artifact for CodeDeploy    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    13.2 CodeBuild — Fully Managed Build Service

Chapter 14: AWS CodePipeline — Orchestrating CI/CD                     🟡 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%) — CodePipeline orchestrates everything
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~2 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: CodePipeline Stages→Actions→Transitions ➜ anihpj
     Complete Pipeline (Source→Build→Deploy to EKS) ➜ Pipeline Security Model
     ➜ Manual Approval Flow — 4 visuals]

    14.1 CodePipeline Architecture
         - Source → Build → Test → Deploy (stages)
         - Actions within stages, transitions between stages
         - Manual approval actions
         - Parallel and sequential stage execution
         [DIAGRAM: CodePipeline — Source → Build → Test → Approve → Deploy stages]

    14.2 Pipeline Integrations
         - Source: CodeCommit, GitHub, Bitbucket, S3, ECR
         - Build: CodeBuild, Jenkins
         - Deploy: CodeDeploy, CloudFormation, ECS, EKS, S3
         - Test: CodeBuild, third-party testing tools
         - Invoke: Lambda, Step Functions
         [TABLE: All CodePipeline action types and integrations]


         [ANIHpj COMPLETE CODEPIPELINE — STAGE-BY-STAGE FLOW]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔄 CODEPIPELINE FOR anihpj — 5 STAGES TO PRODUCTION          │
         │                                                               │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  STAGE 1: SOURCE                      Provider: GitHub  ║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║  git push main → CodePipeline detects change → triggers  ║ │
         │  ║  Artifact: SourceCode.zip (Django project files)         ║ │
         │  ╚═══════════════════════╦══════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │                          │                                     │
         │                          ▼                                     │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  STAGE 2: BUILD                       Provider: CodeBuild║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║  • pip install -r requirements.txt                       ║ │
         │  ║  • python manage.py test (run unit tests)                ║ │
         │  ║  • docker build -t anihpj:v${BUILD_NUM} .                ║ │
         │  ║  • docker push to ECR                                    ║ │
         │  ║  • ECR image scan (check for vulnerabilities)            ║ │
         │  ║  ⏱️  ~7 minutes                                           ║ │
         │  ╚═══════════════════════╦══════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │                          │                                     │
         │                          ▼                                     │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  STAGE 3: DEPLOY TO STAGING       Provider: CodeDeploy   ║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║  • Deploy to EKS staging namespace                        ║ │
         │  ║  • Rolling update (1 Pod at a time)                       ║ │
         │  ║  • Run integration tests (curl /jobs/ endpoint)           ║ │
         │  ║  ⏱️  ~3 minutes                                            ║ │
         │  ╚═══════════════════════╦══════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │                          │                                     │
         │                          ▼                                     │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  STAGE 4: MANUAL APPROVAL                                ║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║  SNS → Slack/Email: "anihpj v${BUILD_NUM} ready in      ║ │
         │  ║  staging. Tests passed. Approve to deploy to prod?"     ║ │
         │  ║  👤 DevOps Engineer reviews and clicks "Approve"         ║ │
         │  ╚═══════════════════════╦══════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │                          │                                     │
         │                          ▼                                     │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  STAGE 5: DEPLOY TO PRODUCTION    Provider: CodeDeploy   ║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║  • Blue/Green deployment to EKS production namespace     ║ │
         │  ║  • Green = new version, Blue = current                    ║ │
         │  ║  • ALB traffic switch: Blue → Green (zero downtime!)     ║ │
         │  ║  • CloudWatch alarm monitors error rate for 10 min       ║ │
         │  ║  • If error rate > 1% → auto-rollback to Blue            ║ │
         │  ║  ⏱️  ~5 minutes (including monitoring period)             ║ │
         │  ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │                                                               │
         │  ⏱️  TOTAL PIPELINE: ~15-20 minutes (end-to-end automated)    │
         │  🔒  SECURITY: IAM Role per stage, KMS-encrypted artifacts   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [CODEDEPLOY BLUE/GREEN ON EKS — TRAFFIC SWITCH DETAIL]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔵🟢 BLUE/GREEN DEPLOYMENT ON EKS — ZERO-DOWNTIME ROLLOUT    │
         │                                                               │
         │  BEFORE DEPLOY (Blue = Production v1.0):                      │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │   👤 Users ──▶ ALB ──▶ Blue Service ──▶ Blue Pods (v1) │ │
         │  │                     (100% traffic)     ┌───┬───┬───┐    │ │
         │  │                                       │v1 │v1 │v1 │    │ │
         │  │                                       └───┴───┴───┘    │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                               │
         │  DURING DEPLOY (Green deployed alongside Blue):               │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │   👤 Users ──▶ ALB ──▶ Blue Service ──▶ Blue Pods (v1) │ │
         │  │                     (100% traffic)     ┌───┬───┬───┐    │ │
         │  │                                       │v1 │v1 │v1 │    │ │
         │  │                                       └───┴───┴───┘    │ │
         │  │                                                          │ │
         │  │              ALB ──▶ Green Service ──▶ Green Pods (v2)  │ │
         │  │                     (0% traffic)       ┌───┬───┬───┐    │ │
         │  │                                       │v2 │v2 │v2 │    │ │
         │  │                                       └───┴───┴───┘    │ │
         │  │                                                          │ │
         │  │              ⏱️  CodeDeploy runs integration tests on    │ │
         │  │                 Green (curl /jobs/, /api/health/)        │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                               │
         │  AFTER TRAFFIC SWITCH (Green = Production v2.0):              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │   👤 Users ──▶ ALB ──▶ Green Service ──▶ Green Pods(v2)│ │
         │  │                     (100% traffic)     ┌───┬───┬───┐    │ │
         │  │                                       │v2 │v2 │v2 │    │ │
         │  │                                       └───┴───┴───┘    │ │
         │  │                                                          │ │
         │  │              Blue Pods (v1) kept for 1 hour              │ │
         │  │              (instant rollback if v2 has issues!)        │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                               │
         │  🚨 ROLLBACK: kubectl rollout undo deploy/anihpj             │
         │     ALB switches back to Blue in SECONDS!                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    15.4 CodeDeploy + EKS Integration
         - Deploy anihpj to EKS using CodeDeploy
         - Blue/Green deployment on EKS
         - Traffic shifting with ALB
         [DIAGRAM: CodeDeploy Blue/Green on EKS — Old → New deployment, traffic switch]
         [CODE: Complete CodeDeploy + EKS deployment configuration]

    15.5 Rollback Strategies
         - Automatic rollback on deployment failure
         - Manual rollback via CodeDeploy
         - kubectl rollout undo (K8s native rollback)
         [COMMAND: kubectl rollout undo deployment/anihpj --to-revision=2]

Chapter 16: CloudFormation — AWS Infrastructure as Code              🟡 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 2 (Config Mgmt 17%) — CloudFormation is THE AWS IaC
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~4 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: CFN Template→Stack→Resources Flow ➜ Intrinsic
     Functions Reference ➜ EKS Cluster CFN Template ➜ StackSets Multi-Account
     ➜ Drift Detection ➜ Change Sets ➜ cfn-init Bootstrapping — 7 visuals]


         [CLOUDFORMATION — FROM TEMPLATE TO STACK TO RESOURCES]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🏗️  CLOUDFORMATION WORKFLOW — DECLARATIVE INFRASTRUCTURE     │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  📝 TEMPLATE (YAML/JSON)                               │    │
         │  │  ─────────────────────────                             │    │
         │  │  AWSTemplateFormatVersion: "2010-09-09"                │    │
         │  │  Description: "anihpj EKS Cluster Stack"               │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  Parameters:  ← User inputs (cluster name, instance    │    │
         │  │                 type, region...)                       │    │
         │  │  Resources:   ← WHAT to create (EKS cluster, VPC,      │    │
         │  │                 Node Groups, IAM roles...)             │    │
         │  │  Outputs:     ← Values returned (cluster endpoint,     │    │
         │  │                 ARN, security group ID...)             │    │
         │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                         │                                     │
         │                         │ aws cloudformation create-stack     │
         │                         ▼                                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  📦 STACK (Logical Group)                              │    │
         │  │  ─────────────────────────                              │    │
         │  │  • One stack = one unit of deployment                  │    │
         │  │  • Create / Update / Delete as one atomic operation    │    │
         │  │  • If creation fails → auto-ROLLBACK (no partial deploys)│   │
         │  │  • Stack name: "anihpj-eks-production"                 │    │
         │  │  • Stack ID: arn:aws:cloudformation:us-east-1:...      │    │
         │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                         │ CloudFormation provisions           │
         │                         ▼ resources in dependency order       │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  ☁️  PHYSICAL RESOURCES                                 │    │
         │  │  ─────────────────────                                  │    │
         │  │  ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐         │    │
         │  │  │  VPC   │ │  EKS   │ │ Node   │ │  IAM   │         │    │
         │  │  │        │ │Cluster │ │ Groups │ │ Roles  │         │    │
         │  │  │10.0.0.0│ │        │ │3 nodes │ │IRSA    │         │    │
         │  │  │  /16   │ │1.29    │ │t3.large│ │OIDC    │         │    │
         │  │  └────────┘ └────────┘ └────────┘ └────────┘         │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  🔄  CHANGE SETS: Preview changes BEFORE applying             │
         │  🔍  DRIFT DETECTION: Find resources modified outside CFN     │
         │  ♻️   ROLLBACK: Auto-revert if CREATE/UPDATE fails             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    16.1 CloudFormation Fundamentals
         - Templates (JSON/YAML), Stacks, StackSets
         - Change Sets: preview before applying
         - Drift Detection: find manual changes
         - Rollback triggers, termination protection
         [DIAGRAM: CloudFormation workflow — Template → Stack → Resources]

    16.2 CloudFormation Template Anatomy
         - AWSTemplateFormatVersion, Description, Metadata, Parameters
         - Mappings, Conditions, Resources, Outputs
         - Intrinsic Functions: !Ref, !Sub, !Join, !Select, !FindInMap
         - Pseudo parameters: AWS::Region, AWS::AccountId, AWS::StackName
         [ANNOTATED TEMPLATE: Each section explained]
         [TABLE: Essential intrinsic functions with examples]

    16.3 CloudFormation + EKS
         - Provisioning EKS clusters with CloudFormation
         - Managed Node Groups
         - VPC, subnets, IAM roles — the full stack
         [CODE: Complete CloudFormation template for EKS cluster + anihpj deployment]

    16.4 StackSets — Multi-Account, Multi-Region
         - Deploy stacks across multiple accounts/regions
         - AWS Organizations integration
         - StackSet operations: create, update, delete

    16.5 CloudFormation Deep Dive — Advanced Topics
         16.5a cfn-init & cfn-signal — Bootstrapping EC2
               - cfn-init: install packages, create files, start services on EC2
               - cfn-signal: signal CloudFormation when instance is ready
               - Wait Conditions & Creation Policy
               [CODE: cfn-init metadata → install Docker, eksctl, awscli on EKS nodes]
         16.5b Nested Stacks — Modular Templates
               - Break large templates into reusable pieces
               - VPC stack → EKS stack → anihpj stack
               - DependsOn for ordering
         16.5c StackSets — Multi-Account Deployments
               - Deploy/update/delete stacks across accounts & OUs
               - Self-managed vs Service-managed permissions
               [USE CASE: Deploy security baseline to ALL AWS accounts in Org]
         16.5d Drift Detection & Change Sets
               - Drift: detect when actual resources differ from template
               - Change Sets: preview what will change before applying
         16.5e Deletion Policy, Stack Policy & Termination Protection
               - DeletionPolicy: Retain, Snapshot, Delete
               - Stack Policy: prevent updates to critical resources
               - Termination Protection on production stacks
         16.5f Custom Resources & Dynamic References
               - Custom Resources: Lambda-backed CloudFormation logic
               - Dynamic References: {{resolve:ssm:/anihpj/db-password}}
               - cfn-hup: auto-reload config on metadata changes
         16.5g Service Role & Capabilities
               - IAM Role for CloudFormation (not your user permissions!)
               - CAPABILITY_IAM, CAPABILITY_NAMED_IAM, CAPABILITY_AUTO_EXPAND

    16.6 CloudFormation Troubleshooting
         - Stack stuck in UPDATE_ROLLBACK_FAILED → ContinueUpdateRollback
         - Rollback triggers — define CloudWatch Alarms to trigger rollback
         - Troubleshooting: check Events tab, CloudTrail, IAM permissions

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

         │                                                               │
         │  ┌────────────────┬────────────────┬────────────────┐        │
         │  │ CLOUDFORMATION │      CDK       │   TERRAFORM    │        │
         │  ├────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────────┤        │
         │  │                │                │                │        │
         │  │  📝 YAML/JSON  │  💻 Real Code  │  📝 HCL Lang   │        │
         │  │                │  (Python/TS)   │                │        │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  │  ┌──────────┐  │  ┌──────────┐  │        │
         │  │  │Resources:│  │  │new eks.   │  │  │resource  │  │        │
         │  │  │  MyVPC:  │  │  │Cluster(   │  │  │"aws_eks_ │  │        │
         │  │  │    Type: │  │  │  this,     │  │  │cluster"  │  │        │
         │  │  │  AWS::..│  │  │  'anihpj', │  │  │"anihpj" {│  │        │
         │  │  │    Cidr: │  │  │  { vpc,   │  │  │  name =  │  │        │
         │  │  │  10.0.. │  │  │    nodes:3 │  │  │  "anihpj"│  │        │
         │  │  └──────────┘  │  │  })        │  │  │  vpc...  │  │        │
         │  │                │  └──────────┘  │  └──────────┘  │        │
         │  │                │                │                │        │
         │  │ ✅ AWS Native  │ ✅ Reuse code  │ ✅ Multi-Cloud │        │
         │  │ ✅ No state    │ ✅ Loops/Ifs   │ ✅ Large       │        │
         │  │    to manage   │ ✅ Unit tests   │    community  │        │
         │  │ ✅ Managed      │ ✅ Higher-level │ ✅ HCP Cloud   │        │
         │  │    rollback    │    constructs  │                │        │
         │  │                │                │                │        │
         │  │ ❌ YAML verbose│ ❌ AWS-only     │ ❌ State mgmt  │        │
         │  │ ❌ 500KB limit │ ❌ Synth step   │ ❌ HCL learning│        │
         │  │ ❌ No loops    │                │    curve      │        │
         │  └────────────────┴────────────────┴────────────────┘        │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 RECOMMENDATION for anihpj:                                │
         │     Start with CDK (Python) → synth to CFN → deploy          │
         │     Use Terraform if you need multi-cloud portability         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         | Abstraction     | Low               | High              | Medium            |

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: AWS Config Resource Tracking ➜ SSM 8 Sub-Services

    19.1 AWS Config

         [SSM SESSION MANAGER vs TRADITIONAL SSH — SECURITY COMPARISON]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔒 SECURE ACCESS TO EC2 — NO OPEN INBOUND PORTS!              │
         │                                                               │
         │  ❌ TRADITIONAL SSH (INSECURE):                                │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  👤 You (laptop)                                         │ │
         │  │       │ SSH (port 22)                                     │ │
         │  │       ▼                                                   │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐               │ │
         │  │  │  🌐 INTERNET (port 22 OPEN to world)  │  ⚠️ ATTACK   │ │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────┐  │    SURFACE!  │ │
         │  │  │  │ Security Group: SSH 0.0.0.0/0  │  │              │ │
         │  │  │  │ (OR your IP — still open port) │  │              │ │
         │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────┘  │               │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────┘               │ │
         │  │                         │                                │ │
         │  │                         ▼                                │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────┐                               │ │
         │  │  │  🖥️  EC2 Instance     │ ← Key pair needed              │ │
         │  │  │  SSH daemon running  │   Keys can be lost/stolen     │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┘                               │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                               │
         │  ✅ SSM SESSION MANAGER (SECURE):                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  👤 You (laptop / AWS Console)                           │ │
         │  │       │ HTTPS (port 443 OUTBOUND ONLY!)                   │ │
         │  │       ▼                                                   │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐               │ │
         │  │  │  🔐 AWS SSM Session Manager           │               │ │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────┐  │               │ │
         │  │  │  │ IAM Authentication + AuthZ     │  │  ✅ NO OPEN   │ │
         │  │  │  │ (Is user ALLOWED to connect?)  │  │    PORTS!    │ │
         │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────┘  │               │ │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────┐  │  ✅ FULL      │ │
         │  │  │  │ CloudTrail Audit Log            │  │    AUDIT     │ │
         │  │  │  │ (Every command logged to S3)    │  │    TRAIL     │ │
         │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────┘  │               │ │
         │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────┐  │  ✅ NO KEY    │ │
         │  │  │  │ Session Logging to CloudWatch   │  │    PAIRS     │ │
         │  │  │  │ or S3 (optional)                │  │    NEEDED    │ │
         │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────┘  │               │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────┘               │ │
         │  │                         │ HTTPS (outbound)                │ │
         │  │                         ▼                                │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────┐                               │ │
         │  │  │  🖥️  EC2 Instance     │ ← SSM Agent (pre-installed)    │ │
         │  │  │  NO SSH daemon       │   NO key pairs                 │ │
         │  │  │  NO open inbound     │   IAM Role grants access       │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┘                               │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 AWS Best Practice: DISABLE SSH, use Session Manager!      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    19.3 Session Manager — No More SSH!
         - Browser-based shell access
         - Full audit trail (CloudTrail + S3 logging)
         - No inbound ports needed (outbound HTTPS only)
         [DIAGRAM: Session Manager — IAM → SSM Agent → Browser shell]

    19.4 Systems Manager Deep Dive — Expanded
         19.4a SSM Documents & Run Command
               - Run shell scripts/PowerShell without SSH
               - Pre-defined AWS documents + custom documents
               - Rate Control, Error Control for bulk operations
         19.4b SSM Automation — Runbooks
               - Pre-defined runbooks (AWS-*, e.g., AWS-RestartEC2Instance)
               - Custom automation documents for incident response
               [EXAMPLE: Auto-remediation runbook — stop unhealthy anihpj instance]
         19.4c SSM Patch Manager & Maintenance Windows
               - Scan and install OS patches on schedule
               - Patch baselines, maintenance windows, patch compliance
         19.4d SSM Session Manager — No More SSH
               - Browser-based shell to EC2 (no open inbound ports!)
               - Full audit via CloudTrail + S3 + CloudWatch Logs
               - VPC Endpoints for Session Manager
               - Hybrid environments (on-premises servers)
         19.4e SSM OpsCenter & OpsItems
               - Aggregated view of operational issues
               - OpsItems linked to CloudWatch Alarms, Config Rules, EventBridge
         19.4f SSM Compliance, Inventory & State Manager
               - Track patch compliance, software inventory
               - State Manager: enforce desired configuration state
         19.4g SSM Default Host Management Configuration (DHMC)
         19.4h SSM Hybrid Environments & IoT Greengrass Integration

Chapter 19B: AWS Elastic Beanstalk — Managed Application Platform  🟡 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19B.1 Elastic Beanstalk Overview
         - Platform-as-a-Service: upload code, EB handles everything else
         - Supported platforms: Python, Node.js, Java, .NET, Go, Docker, PHP, Ruby
         - EB vs ECS vs EKS vs Lambda — when to use which
         - Components: Application, Environment, Environment Tier (Web/Worker)
         [DIAGRAM: Elastic Beanstalk architecture — ALB → ASG → EC2 with your app]

    19B.2 Deploying anihpj on Elastic Beanstalk
         - eb init, eb create, eb deploy
         - .ebextensions for custom configuration
         - Environment configuration: capacity, load balancer, rolling updates
         [CODE: .ebextensions/django.config for anihpj]
         [CODE: eb CLI workflow — init → create → deploy → open]

    19B.3 Elastic Beanstalk Deployment Modes
         - All at Once (fastest, brief downtime)
         - Rolling (batched replacement)
         - Rolling with Additional Batch (no capacity loss)
         - Immutable (new ASG, swap all at once — safest)
         - Traffic Splitting (canary — new instances, split traffic)
         - Blue/Green (clone environment, swap URLs)
         [COMPARISON TABLE: 6 deployment modes — downtime, speed, rollback ease]

    19B.4 Elastic Beanstalk High Availability
         - Multi-AZ deployment, Auto Scaling
         - Load-balanced environment (Web Server tier)
         - Worker tier: SQS-driven processing
         - Environment cloning for Blue/Green

    19B.5 Elastic Beanstalk Extensions & Troubleshooting
         - Lifecycle policies, environment variables
         - Platform updates, managed updates
         - Troubleshooting: EB logs, Health Dashboard, CloudWatch integration

Chapter 20: Amazon EKS — Managed Kubernetes on AWS                   🟡 ☸️ 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 1 (SDLC 22%) & Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%) — EKS is K8s on AWS
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~4 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: EKS Architecture (AWS CP + Your Data Plane) ➜
     VPC CNI (ENI→Secondary IPs→Pods) ➜ Managed vs Self-Managed Node Groups
     ➜ aws-auth ConfigMap + IRSA Flow ➜ EKS Add-ons Architecture — 5 visuals]


         [EKS ARCHITECTURE — AWS-MANAGED CONTROL PLANE + YOUR DATA PLANE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ☸️  AMAZON EKS — WHO MANAGES WHAT?                            │
         │                                                               │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  AWS-MANAGED (You DON'T touch this!)                     ║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  ║ │
         │  ║  │           ☸️  CONTROL PLANE (3 AZs, HA)              │  ║ │
         │  ║  │                                                      │  ║ │
         │  ║  │  ┌──────────┐ ┌────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌─────────────┐ │  ║ │
         │  ║  │  │API Server│ │etcd│ │Scheduler │ │Controller Mgr│ │  ║ │
         │  ║  │  │(AWS runs)│ │(AWS│ │(AWS runs)│ │(AWS runs)    │ │  ║ │
         │  ║  │  └──────────┘ │runs│ └──────────┘ └─────────────┘ │  ║ │
         │  ║  │               └────┘                                │  ║ │
         │  ║  │  💰 $0.10/hour per cluster (flat fee)               │  ║ │
         │  ║  │  🔄 AWS auto-upgrades, auto-scales, auto-heals      │  ║ │
         │  ║  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  ║ │
         │  ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │                          │                                     │
         │                          │ kubelet connects to API Server       │
         │                          ▼                                     │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  YOU-MANAGED (You configure, AWS provides EC2)          ║ │
         │  ║  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────║ │
         │  ║                                                           ║ │
         │  ║  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐        ║ │
         │  ║  │  Worker Node 1      │  │  Worker Node 2      │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  (EC2 t3.large)     │  │  (EC2 t3.large)     │        ║ │
         │  ║  │                     │  │                     │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  ┌───────────────┐  │  │  ┌───────────────┐  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  │ kubelet       │  │  │  │ kubelet       │  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  │ kube-proxy    │  │  │  │ kube-proxy    │  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  │ containerd    │  │  │  │ containerd    │  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  │ VPC CNI       │  │  │  │ VPC CNI       │  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  └───────────────┘  │  │  └───────────────┘  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │                     │  │                     │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  ┌───────────────┐  │  │  ┌───────────────┐  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  │ anihpj Pod 📦 │  │  │  │ anihpj Pod 📦 │  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  │ IP: 10.0.1.45 │  │  │  │ IP: 10.0.2.73 │  │        ║ │
         │  ║  │  └───────────────┘  │  │  └───────────────┘  │        ║ │
         │  ║  └─────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘        ║ │
         │  ║                                                           ║ │
         │  ║  💰 You pay for EC2 instances only (or Fargate vCPU/RAM) ║ │
         │  ║  🔧 You manage: AMI updates, scaling, security patches    ║ │
         │  ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝ │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    20.1 What is Amazon EKS?
         - AWS-managed Kubernetes control plane
         - HA control plane across 3 AZs (AWS manages master nodes)
         - You manage worker nodes + applications
         - EKS vs self-managed K8s on EC2
         - EKS vs ECS decision guide
         [COMPARISON TABLE: EKS vs self-managed K8s vs ECS vs AKS vs GKE]
         [DIAGRAM: EKS architecture — AWS-managed CP + customer-managed data plane]

    20.2 EKS Cluster Creation — Multiple Approaches
         - eksctl: the official CLI (simplest)
         - AWS Console: GUI wizard
         - CloudFormation/CDK/Terraform: IaC approach
         - eksctl create cluster --name anihpj-cluster --region us-east-1
         [CODE: eksctl cluster config YAML with all options]
         [CODE: Equivalent CloudFormation and Terraform snippets]

    20.3 EKS Networking Deep Dive
         - VPC CNI plugin: Pods get real VPC IPs (not overlay network)
         - IP address planning: how many IPs per node?
         - Custom networking (ENIConfig, Security Groups per Pod)
         - Prefix delegation mode (more Pods per node)
         [DIAGRAM: VPC CNI — ENI → Secondary IPs → Pods]

    20.4 EKS Node Groups
         - Managed Node Groups: AWS manages scaling, updates, termination
         - Self-Managed Node Groups: full control, more responsibility
         - Launch templates, AMI selection, bootstrap script
         - Custom AMIs (Bottlerocket, Ubuntu, Amazon Linux)
         [TABLE: Managed vs Self-managed node groups comparison]
         [CODE: Node group configuration for anihpj (mixed instances, spot)]

    20.5 EKS Authentication & Authorization
         - aws-auth ConfigMap: map IAM roles to K8s RBAC groups
         - aws eks update-kubeconfig --name cluster --region us-east-1
         - IRSA (IAM Roles for Service Accounts) — revisited for EKS
         [DIAGRAM: IAM → aws-auth ConfigMap → K8s RBAC → Pod access]
         [CODE: Complete RBAC setup for anihpj team]

    20.6 EKS Add-ons — Essential Components
         - VPC CNI (networking)
         - CoreDNS (service discovery)
         - kube-proxy (network rules)
         - AWS Load Balancer Controller (ALB/NLB integration)
         - EBS CSI Driver (persistent volumes)
         - EFS CSI Driver (shared file storage)
         - CloudWatch Agent (container insights)
         [TABLE: EKS add-ons — mandatory vs recommended vs optional]
         [DIAGRAM: Add-on architecture on EKS]

    20.7 EKS Anywhere & EKS Distro
         - EKS Anywhere: EKS on-premises
         - EKS Distro: the open-source distribution
         - EKS on Outposts, Local Zones, Wavelength

Chapter 22: EKS Scaling & Availability                                 🟡 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%) — Autoscaling is a core competency
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: HPA (Metrics→Scale) ➜ Cluster Autoscaler vs Karpenter
     ➜ Multi-AZ Pod Distribution ➜ Spot Interruption Handling Flow ➜ PDB
     Protection — 5 scaling diagrams]

    22.1 Horizontal Pod Autoscaling (HPA)
         - Scale Pods based on CPU/Memory/Custom metrics
         - HPA + Metric Server + CloudWatch metrics
         - minReplicas, maxReplicas, target utilization
         [DIAGRAM: HPA — Metric Server → HPA → Deployment (scale up/down)]
         [CODE: HPA YAML for anihpj]

    22.2 Vertical Pod Autoscaling (VPA)
         - Adjust Pod resource requests/limits automatically
         - VPA recommender, updater, admission controller
         - VPA vs HPA — use both!


         [HPA + CLUSTER AUTOSCALER — TWO-LEVEL AUTOSCALING]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  📈 AUTOSCALING ON EKS — PODS SCALE + NODES SCALE             │
         │                                                               │
         │  LEVEL 1: HORIZONTAL POD AUTOSCALER (HPA)                     │
         │  ─────────────────────────────────────────                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  CloudWatch / Metrics Server                          │    │
         │  │  │ "anihpj CPU = 85% (target: 50%)"                   │    │
         │  │  │                                                     │    │
         │  │  ▼                                                     │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐      ┌──────────────────────┐          │    │
         │  │  │   HPA    │─────▶│ Deployment: anihpj    │          │    │
         │  │  │          │      │ replicas: 3 → 5       │          │    │
         │  │  │ min: 3   │      │                       │          │    │
         │  │  │ max: 10  │      │ ┌────┬────┬────┬────┐ │          │    │
         │  │  │ CPU: 50% │      │ │ P1 │ P2 │ P3 │ P4 │ │          │    │
         │  │  └──────────┘      │ │ P5 │    │    │    │ │          │    │
         │  │                    │ └────┴────┴────┴────┘ │          │    │
         │  │                    └──────────────────────┘          │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  ⚠️  PROBLEM: HPA creates 2 new Pods, but Node is FULL!        │
         │     Pod status: PENDING (no node has enough capacity)         │
         │                                                               │
         │  LEVEL 2: CLUSTER AUTOSCALER (CA)                             │
         │  ────────────────────────────────────                          │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────┐                                 │    │
         │  │  │Cluster Autoscaler│ "2 Pods PENDING! Need more      │    │
         │  │  │                  │  nodes!"                         │    │
         │  │  └────────┬─────────┘                                  │    │
         │  │           │                                             │    │
         │  │           │ aws autoscaling set-desired-capacity       │    │
         │  │           ▼                                             │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────┐     ┌──────────────────┐        │    │
         │  │  │ Auto Scaling     │────▶│ New EC2 Instance  │        │    │
         │  │  │ Group            │     │ (Node 3)          │        │    │
         │  │  │ desired: 2 → 3   │     │ t3.large          │        │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────┘     └────────┬─────────┘        │    │
         │  │                                     │                   │    │
         │  │                                     │ kubelet registers │    │
         │  │                                     ▼                   │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
         │  │  │  Scheduler assigns PENDING Pods to new Node 3    │  │    │
         │  │  │  Pod P4 → Node 3 ✅  |  Pod P5 → Node 3 ✅       │  │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  ⏱️  TOTAL TIME: ~2-3 minutes (60s node boot + Pod start)     │
         │  🆚  KARPENTER: Next-gen alternative, provisions nodes        │
         │     directly (bypasses ASG), faster, more flexible            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    22.3 Cluster Autoscaler
         - Auto-scale worker nodes based on pending Pods
         - Works with ASG (Auto Scaling Groups)
         - Karpenter: next-gen faster, more flexible autoscaler
         [DIAGRAM: Cluster Autoscaler → ASG (add/remove nodes)]
         [TABLE: Cluster Autoscaler vs Karpenter comparison]

    22.4 EKS HA Best Practices
         - Multi-AZ node distribution
         - Pod anti-affinity (spread Pods across nodes/zones)
         - Pod Disruption Budgets (PDB)
         - Topology Spread Constraints
         [CODE: PDB YAML for anihpj — maxUnavailable during voluntary disruptions]

    22.5 Spot Instances for EKS
         - 60-90% cost savings vs On-Demand
         - Spot interruption handling (2-minute notice)
         - AWS Node Termination Handler
         - Mixed instance policies in ASG
         [DIAGRAM: Spot interruption flow — notice → cordon → drain → terminate]

Chapter 23: EKS Security — Defense in Depth                            🟡 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 6 (Security 17%) — EKS security is multi-layered
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: Defense in Depth Layers ➜ NetworkPolicy Firewall
     Rules ➜ KMS Envelope Encryption ➜ ECR Image Scanning Pipeline ➜
     GuardDuty + Security Hub Integration — 5 security diagrams]

    23.1 EKS Security Model
         - Shared responsibility for EKS
         - Security groups: control plane, node groups, Pods
         - Pod Security Standards (PSS): Privileged, Baseline, Restricted
         - Pod Security Admission (replaces PSP)
         [DIAGRAM: EKS security layers — VPC → SG → IAM → RBAC → PSS → NetworkPolicy]

    23.2 Network Policies
         - Kubernetes-native firewall rules
         - Ingress/Egress rules based on Pod selectors, namespace selectors, IP blocks
         - Calico for advanced network policy features
         [CODE: NetworkPolicy — allow anihpj only from ALB, allow DB only from anihpj]

    23.3 Secrets Encryption
         - KMS envelope encryption for K8s Secrets at rest
         - AWS KMS key for EKS cluster
         - Secrets Manager + External Secrets Operator

    23.4 Image Security
         - ECR image scanning (basic + enhanced)
         - Image signing with AWS Signer
         - Admission control: verify image signatures before running

    23.5 Runtime Security
         - Falco: runtime threat detection
         - GuardDuty EKS Protection
         - Security Hub integration

    23.6 Audit Logging
         - EKS control plane logs → CloudWatch Logs
         - API server audit logs, authenticator logs
         - CloudTrail for AWS API calls

         │  │  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘            │    │

         │  │       └─────────────┼─────────────┘                   │    │
         │  │                     │                                  │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌───┴────┐  ┌──────────┐             │    │
         │  │  │ App Logs │  │Custom  │  │  Lambda  │             │    │
         │  │  │          │  │Metrics │  │          │             │    │
         │  │  │ "ERROR:  │  │"anihpj │  │Invoc:500│             │    │
         │  │  │  DB conn"│  │ orders"│  │Dur: 80ms│             │    │
         │  │  └────┬─────┘  └────┬────┘  └────┬─────┘            │    │
         │  │       └─────────────┼─────────────┘                   │    │
         │  └─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                        │                                      │
         │                        ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │

         │  │  ───────────────────────────────────────               │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐            │    │
         │  │  │ METRICS  │  │  LOGS    │  │  ALARMS  │            │    │
         │  │  │          │  │          │  │          │            │    │
         │  │  │Namespace │  │Log Groups│  │CPU > 80% │            │    │
         │  │  │: AWS/EKS │  │: /aws/eks│  │→ SNS     │            │    │
         │  │  │          │  │          │  │→ Lambda  │            │    │
         │  │  │Dimension │  │Log Stream│  │→ ASG     │            │    │
         │  │  │: Cluster │  │: per-Pod │  │   action │            │    │
         │  │  │          │  │          │  │          │            │    │
         │  │  │Statistic │  │Logs      │  │Composite │            │    │
         │  │  │: Average │  │Insights  │  │ Alarms    │            │    │
         │  │  │          │  │(SQL-like │  │(A AND B) │            │    │
         │  │  │Period:   │  │ queries) │  │          │            │    │
         │  │  │1 minute  │  │          │  │Anomaly   │            │    │
         │  │  └──────────┘  └──────────┘  │Detection │            │    │
         │  │                               └──────────┘            │    │
         │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘    │

         │                         ▼                                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │

         │  │  ────────────────────────                               │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐            │    │
         │  │  │Dashboard │  │   SNS    │  │  Lambda  │            │    │
         │  │  │          │  │          │  │          │            │    │

         │  │  │Error rate│  │→ Slack   │  │  Pods    │            │    │
         │  │  │Latency   │  │→ PagerDty│  │→ Restart │            │    │
         │  │  │ heatmap  │  │→ SMS     │  │  Service │            │    │

    25.1 CloudWatch Metrics
         - Namespace, dimensions, timestamps, statistics
         - Standard metrics (free, 5-min) vs Detailed monitoring (paid, 1-min)
         - Custom metrics (PutMetricData API)
         - Metric Math: create derived metrics from existing ones
         [DIAGRAM: CloudWatch metrics pipeline — source → namespace → statistic → alarm]

    25.2 CloudWatch Logs
         - Log groups, log streams, retention policies
         - Logs Insights: SQL-like query language
         - Metric Filters: extract metrics from logs
         - Subscription Filters: stream logs to Lambda, Kinesis, OpenSearch
         [CODE: CloudWatch Logs Insights queries for anihpj debugging]

    25.3 CloudWatch Alarms
         - Metric alarms, composite alarms
         - Alarm states: OK, ALARM, INSUFFICIENT_DATA
         - Alarm actions: SNS, Auto Scaling, EC2, Lambda
         [CODE: CloudWatch alarm for anihpj CPU > 80% → SNS → PagerDuty]

    25.4 CloudWatch Dashboards
         - Custom dashboards with widgets
         - Cross-account, cross-region dashboards
         - Automatic dashboards (service dashboards)

    25.6 CloudWatch Synthetics & Advanced Features
         - CloudWatch Synthetics (Canaries): scripted browser checks
         - CloudWatch Anomaly Detection: ML-based metric anomaly detection
         - CloudWatch Logs Live Tail: real-time log streaming
         - CloudWatch Agent: unified logs + metrics collector (replaces old logs agent)

    25.7 CloudWatch + EKS
         - Container Insights: metrics, logs, traces
         - CloudWatch Agent for EKS
         - Fluent Bit → CloudWatch Logs
         - Control plane logging (API server, audit, etc.)
         [DIAGRAM: EKS → Fluent Bit → CloudWatch Logs → Insights]

     CloudTrail Lake SQL Analytics ➜ Config Rules→Remediation Flow — 3 visuals]

         - API activity logging: who did what, when, from where

         - Organization trail (all accounts)
         - CloudTrail Lake (SQL queries on trails)
         [DIAGRAM: CloudTrail architecture — API → Trail → S3/CloudWatch Logs]

    26.2 CloudTrail vs CloudWatch vs Config
         [COMPARISON TABLE]
         | Service     | What it tracks     | Use Case                    |
         | CloudTrail  | API calls (Who?)   | Audit, security forensics   |
         | CloudWatch  | Metrics/Logs (How?)| Performance, alerting       |
         | Config      | Resource history   | Compliance, drift detection |
         |             | (What changed?)    |                             |

    26.3 AWS Config Deep Dive
         - Configuration recorder, delivery channel
         - Managed rules + custom (Lambda) rules
         - Remediation actions (SSM Automation)
         - Conformance packs (collections of rules)

Chapter 27: AWS X-Ray & Distributed Tracing                            🟡 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 4 (Monitoring 15%) — Distributed tracing for microservices
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~1.5 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: X-Ray Trace Anatomy (Segments→Subsegments) ➜
     Service Map Visualization ➜ X-Ray DaemonSet on EKS ➜ ServiceLens — 4 visuals]


         [AWS X-RAY — DISTRIBUTED TRACING FOR anihpj ON EKS]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔍 X-RAY TRACE — FOLLOW A REQUEST THROUGH ALL SERVICES       │
         │                                                               │
         │  👤 User clicks "Search Jobs" on anihpj                       │
         │       │                                                       │
         │       ▼                                                       │
         │  ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗ │
         │  ║  TRACE ID: 1-5f132c8e-...  (end-to-end request journey)  ║ │
         │  ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝ │
         │       │                                                       │
         │       ▼                                                       │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  SEGMENT 1: ALB (Load Balancer)                         │ │
         │  │  ─────────────────────────────                           │ │
         │  │  Duration: 2ms                                            │ │
         │  │  Status: 200                                              │ │
         │  │  Annotations: target=anihpj-prod, az=us-east-1a          │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                            │                                  │
         │                            ▼                                  │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  SEGMENT 2: anihpj Django App (EKS Pod)                 │ │
         │  │  ──────────────────────────────────────                   │ │
         │  │  Duration: 450ms                                          │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │ │
         │  │  │  SUBSEGMENT 2a: Django Middleware                 │   │ │
         │  │  │  Duration: 5ms | Auth check: PASSED ✅            │   │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │ │
         │  │  │  SUBSEGMENT 2b: Django View (jobpost.views.search)│   │ │
         │  │  │  Duration: 20ms | Query params: skill=Python      │   │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │ │
         │  │  │  SUBSEGMENT 2c: RDS PostgreSQL Query              │   │ │
         │  │  │  Duration: 400ms ⚠️  SLOW!                        │   │ │
         │  │  │  SQL: SELECT * FROM jobpost_jobpost WHERE...      │   │ │
         │  │  │  Metadata: table=jobpost_jobpost, rows=1523       │   │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │ │
         │  │  │  SUBSEGMENT 2d: S3 (static files)                 │   │ │
         │  │  │  Duration: 25ms | File: /static/css/main.css      │   │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                               │
         │  🔍 SERVICE MAP (X-Ray Console):                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │
         │  │  ALB ◀── anihpj ◀──┬── RDS (400ms avg) ⚠️        │        │
         │  │  (2ms)    (450ms)  └── S3 (25ms avg)            │        │
         │  │                                                      │        │
         │  │  👆 Click RDS node → see: "Missing index on          │        │
         │  │     jobpost_jobpost.skills column → Add index!"     │        │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 X-Ray tells you WHERE the bottleneck is — not just THAT   │
         │     there's a bottleneck!                                     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    27.1 X-Ray Fundamentals

         [DIAGRAM: X-Ray trace — Client → ALB → EKS Pod → RDS → response]

Chapter 29: AWS Security Services Deep Dive                            🔴 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 6 (Security 17%) — Security is 17% of the exam
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~4 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: KMS Key Hierarchy ➜ Secrets Manager Auto-Rotation
     ➜ GuardDuty Threat Detection Flow ➜ Security Hub Centralized Dashboard
     ➜ Inspector Scanning Pipeline ➜ WAF+Shield Defense Layers — 6 visuals]


         [AWS KMS — ENVELOPE ENCRYPTION EXPLAINED]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔐 HOW KMS ENCRYPTS YOUR DATA — ENVELOPE ENCRYPTION          │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  STEP 1: CREATE A MASTER KEY (CMK)                    │    │
         │  │  ──────────────────────────────────                    │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
         │  │  │  🔑 Customer Master Key (CMK)                    │  │    │
         │  │  │  ─────────────────────────────                   │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Stored IN KMS (NEVER leaves AWS)             │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Used to ENCRYPT/DECRYPT data keys            │  │    │
         │  │  │  • NEVER used to encrypt actual data            │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Can be: AWS-managed OR Customer-managed      │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Customer-managed: YOU control the key policy │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Supports automatic yearly rotation           │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Multi-Region keys available                  │  │    │
         │  │  └────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘  │    │
         │  │                       │                                 │    │
         │  │                       │ GenerateDataKey                 │    │
         │  │                       ▼                                 │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
         │  │  │  📄 DATA KEY (Plaintext + Encrypted)             │  │    │
         │  │  │  ─────────────────────────────────                │  │    │
         │  │  │  KMS returns TWO versions of the same key:       │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Plaintext key → use to encrypt YOUR data     │  │    │
         │  │  │  • Encrypted key → store WITH your data          │  │    │
         │  │  └────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘  │    │
         │  │                       │                                 │    │
         │  │                       │ Encrypt with plaintext key      │    │
         │  │                       ▼                                 │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
         │  │  │  💾 YOUR DATA (Encrypted)                         │  │    │
         │  │  │  ────────────────────────                          │  │    │
         │  │  │  ┌──────────────────────┐ ┌────────────────────┐ │  │    │
         │  │  │  │ Encrypted Data       │ │ Encrypted Data Key │ │  │    │
         │  │  │  │ (Your actual files,  │ │ (Wrapped by CMK)   │ │  │    │
         │  │  │  │  DB, secrets, etc.)  │ │                     │ │  │    │
         │  │  │  └──────────────────────┘ └────────────────────┘ │  │    │
         │  │  │  BOTH stored together (S3, EBS, RDS, Secrets Mgr)│  │    │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  TO DECRYPT:                                            │    │
         │  │  1. Send encrypted data key → KMS                       │    │
         │  │  2. KMS decrypts it using CMK                           │    │
         │  │  3. Use plaintext data key to decrypt your data         │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  💡 CMK NEVER leaves KMS. Only data keys travel.        │    │
         │  │     This is called ENVELOPE ENCRYPTION.                 │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    29.1 KMS (Key Management Service)
         - Customer Managed Keys (CMK) vs AWS Managed Keys
         - Key policies, grants, rotation
         - Multi-Region keys
         - KMS + EKS: envelope encryption for Secrets
         [DIAGRAM: KMS key hierarchy — CMK → Data Keys → Encrypted Data]

    29.2 Secrets Manager
         - Store and rotate secrets automatically
         - RDS, DocumentDB, Redshift auto-rotation
         - Cross-account access
         - Secrets Manager + EKS via External Secrets Operator
         [CODE: Secret rotation Lambda template]

    29.3 GuardDuty
         - Threat detection: ML-based anomaly detection
         - Findings: reconnaissance, instance compromise, data exfiltration
         - GuardDuty EKS Protection (K8s audit log monitoring)
         - GuardDuty + EventBridge → Lambda auto-remediation

    29.4 Security Hub
         - Centralized security findings dashboard
         - Standards: CIS AWS Foundations, PCI DSS, NIST
         - Automated remediation with custom actions

    29.5 Inspector
         - Vulnerability scanning: EC2, ECR, Lambda
         - CIS benchmarks, CVE detection
         - Network reachability analysis

    29.6 WAF & Shield
         - WAF: web application firewall, rule-based filtering
         - Shield Standard (free, L3/L4) vs Shield Advanced (paid, L7 + DRT)
         - WAF + ALB/CloudFront/API Gateway

Chapter 32: Secure CI/CD — DevSecOps                                    🔴 🎯

     Scanning Flow ➜ Image Signing + Admission Control ➜ WAF+Shield Protection
     Layers ➜ Vulnerability Management Lifecycle — 5 DevSecOps diagrams]

    32.1 Shift Left Security
         - SAST (Static Analysis): SonarQube, CodeGuru Reviewer
         - DAST (Dynamic Analysis): OWASP ZAP, penetration testing
         - SCA (Software Composition Analysis): dependency scanning
         [DIAGRAM: Security gates in CI/CD pipeline]

    32.2 CodePipeline Security
         - IAM roles per stage (least privilege)
         - Artifact encryption (KMS)
         - Approval gates for production deployments

    32.3 Image Security in CI/CD

         - Image signing (AWS Signer)
         - Admission control in EKS (verify signatures)
         [CODE: buildspec.yml — image scan + sign before push]

    32.4 Vulnerability Management
         - Amazon Inspector continuous scanning (EC2, ECR, Lambda)
         - Inspector + SSM Agent for EC2 vulnerability assessment
         - GuardDuty Advanced: Malware Protection, EKS Protection, RDS Protection, S3 Protection, Lambda Protection
         - AWS Detective: investigate and visualize security findings
         - Security Hub → EventBridge → Lambda auto-remediation
         - Finding lifecycle: NEW → NOTIFIED → RESOLVED → SUPPRESSED

    32.5 AWS WAF & Shield — Application Protection
         - WAF: Web ACLs, rules (IP sets, rate-based, managed rules)
         - WAF + ALB / CloudFront / API Gateway / AppSync
         - Shield Standard (free, L3/L4) vs Shield Advanced ($3K/mo, L7 + DRT)

Chapter 32B: AWS Organizations, Control Tower & IAM Identity Center 🔴 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    32B.1 AWS Organizations Deep Dive
         - Management account, member accounts, organizational units (OUs)
         - Service Control Policies (SCPs): Preventive guardrails
         - SCP evaluation: Explicit DENY > ALLOW (even if IAM allows it)
         - Tag Policies, Backup Policies, AI Opt-out Policies
         [DIAGRAM: Org structure — Root → OU (Prod, Dev, Security) → Accounts]

    32B.2 AWS Control Tower
         - Landing Zone: automated multi-account setup
         - Account Factory: provision new accounts with guardrails
         - Preventive (SCP) + Detective (Config Rules) guardrails
         - Customizations for AWS Control Tower (CfCT): GitOps for accounts
         - Account Factory for Terraform (AFT)
         - Control Tower + AWS Config integration (organizational rules)
         [DIAGRAM: Control Tower — Landing Zone → OU Structure → Guardrails]

    32B.3 IAM Identity Center (formerly AWS SSO)
         - Centralized access management across ALL accounts
         - Identity sources: Built-in, Active Directory, external IdP (Okta, Azure AD)
         - Permission Sets: collections of IAM policies
         - Assign users/groups to accounts with permission sets
         - Unified portal for multi-account access

    32B.4 Multi-Account Strategy Best Practices
         - Security (Audit, Log Archive) + Workloads (Dev, Staging, Prod)
         - AWS Health Dashboard: service health + personal health events
         - EventBridge integration for health events (auto-remediation)
         - EC2 Instance Status Checks: System (AWS fixes) vs Instance (you fix)

    🏋️  Chapter 32B Practice Drill: Set Up Multi-Account Org
    📝  Chapter 32B Practice Questions (10 Questions)

    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

     ➜ SQS DLQ Architecture ➜ Step Functions State Machine — 4 visuals]


         [EVENTBRIDGE — EVENT-DRIVEN ARCHITECTURE FOR anihpj]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ⚡ EVENTBRIDGE — REACT TO ANYTHING IN YOUR AWS ENVIRONMENT    │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  EVENT SOURCES (What triggers events)                 │    │
         │  │  ────────────────────────────────────                  │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌─────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │AWS Events│ │Custom App│ │Scheduled │ │3rd Party│ │    │
         │  │  │          │ │Events    │ │Events    │ │(SaaS)   │ │    │
         │  │  │EC2 state │ │"anihpj   │ │Every hour│ │Datadog  │ │    │
         │  │  │ change   │ │ order    │ │cron(0 *  │ │PagerDuty│ │    │
         │  │  │CodeDeploy│ │ placed"  │ │ * * ? *) │ │Auth0    │ │    │
         │  │  │ status   │ │          │ │          │ │         │ │    │
         │  │  │RDS backup│ │Custom    │ │Rate      │ │Partner  │ │    │
         │  │  │ complete │ │Event Bus │ │Express.  │ │Event Bus│ │    │
         │  │  └────┬─────┘ └────┬─────┘ └────┬─────┘ └────┬────┘ │    │
         │  │       │            │            │            │       │    │
         │  │       └────────────┼────────────┼────────────┘       │    │
         │  │                    │            │                     │    │
         │  └────────────────────┼────────────┼─────────────────────┘    │
         │                       │            │                          │
         │                       ▼            ▼                          │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  🎯 EVENTBRIDGE EVENT BUS                             │    │
         │  │  ─────────────────────────                             │    │
         │  │  • Ingests events from all sources                     │    │
         │  │  • Filters events by RULES (pattern matching)         │    │
         │  │  • Routes to TARGETS                                   │    │
         │  │  • Schema Registry: discover event structure           │    │
         │  │  • Event Replay: replay past events (debugging)       │    │
         │  │  • Cross-account: send events to other AWS accounts   │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │    │
         │  │  │  RULE: "If source=aws.ec2 AND state=terminated" │  │    │
         │  │  │  TARGETS: ─┬── Lambda: update inventory DB      │  │    │
         │  │  │            ├── SNS: notify ops team              │  │    │
         │  │  │            ├── SQS: queue for processing         │  │    │
         │  │  │            ├── Step Functions: run cleanup       │  │    │
         │  │  │            └── CloudWatch Logs: store event      │  │    │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 EVENTBRIDGE for anihpj:                                    │
         │     • New job posted → EventBridge → Lambda (index for search)│
         │     • EC2 spot interruption → EventBridge → cordon Node      │
         │     • CodeDeploy failure → EventBridge → Slack notification   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    33.1 EventBridge (formerly CloudWatch Events)

         - Default bus, custom bus, cross-account
         - Schema registry, event replay

    33.2 SNS (Simple Notification Service)
         - Pub/sub messaging, topics, subscriptions
         - Message filtering (subscription filter policies)
         - Fanout pattern: SNS → multiple SQS queues
         [DIAGRAM: SNS fanout — 1 message → SNS → 5 queues (each for different team)]

    33.3 SQS (Simple Queue Service)
         - Standard vs FIFO queues
         - Dead Letter Queues (DLQ)
         - Visibility timeout, long polling
         - Delay queues, message timers

    33.4 Step Functions
         - State machine workflows
         - Standard vs Express workflows
         - Error handling: retry, catch, fallback
         - Integration with 200+ AWS services

Chapter 34: Incident Response & Disaster Recovery                     🔴 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 5 (Incident Mgmt 14%) & Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%)
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: Incident Response Lifecycle ➜ Auto-Remediation
     Patterns ➜ DR Spectrum (RPO vs RTO vs Cost) ➜ Velero EKS Backup to S3
     ➜ Chaos Engineering with AWS FIS — 5 incident response diagrams]

    34.1 Incident Response Plan
         - Detection → Analysis → Containment → Eradication → Recovery
         - CloudWatch Alarms → SNS → Lambda (auto-remediation)
         - Runbooks: SSM Automation Documents
         [DIAGRAM: Incident response flow — alarm → notification → runbook → recovery]

    34.2 Auto-Remediation Patterns
         - EC2 instance unhealthy → ASG replaces it
         - Pod crash → K8s restarts it
         - Security finding → Lambda blocks IP
         - SSL cert expiring → ACM auto-renews
         [TABLE: Failure scenarios and auto-remediation mappings]


         [DISASTER RECOVERY STRATEGIES — RTO vs RPO vs COST SPECTRUM]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🛡️  DR STRATEGY COMPARISON — CHOOSE BASED ON BUSINESS NEEDS   │
         │                                                               │
         │  COST $                     RTO (Recovery Time)               │
         │   │                         ──────────────────                │
         │   │                                                           │
         │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │  │  MULTI-SITE ACTIVE/ACTIVE ($$$$$)                  │    │
         │   │  │  ─────────────────────────────────                 │    │
         │   │  │  • Two regions running simultaneously              │    │
         │   │  │  • Route 53 routes users to nearest healthy       │    │
         │   │  │  • RTO: < 1 minute | RPO: Near zero               │    │
         │   │  │  • Data: DynamoDB Global Tables, Aurora Global DB │    │
         │   │  │  • Use: Mission-critical apps (payment systems)   │    │
         │   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │   │                                                           │
         │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │  │  WARM STANDBY ($$$)                                │    │
         │   │  │  ─────────────────                                 │    │
         │   │  │  • Scaled-down copy running in DR region           │    │
         │   │  │  • On disaster: scale UP → switch DNS             │    │
         │   │  │  • RTO: 5-15 minutes | RPO: Seconds               │    │
         │   │  │  • Uses: ASG (min=1), RDS read replica promoted   │    │
         │   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │   │                                                           │
         │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │  │  PILOT LIGHT ($$)                                  │    │
         │   │  │  ───────────────                                    │    │
         │   │  │  • Minimal core services running in DR             │    │
         │   │  │  • On disaster: provision full environment        │    │
         │   │  │  • RTO: 10-30 minutes | RPO: Minutes              │    │
         │   │  │  • Uses: CloudFormation templates ready, data     │    │
         │   │  │    replicated (RDS cross-region, S3 CRR)          │    │
         │   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │   │                                                           │
         │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │  │  BACKUP & RESTORE ($)                              │    │
         │   │  │  ────────────────────                               │    │
         │   │  │  • Regular backups to S3 (cross-region)            │    │
         │   │  │  • On disaster: restore from backups              │    │
         │   │  │  • RTO: Hours | RPO: Hours                        │    │
         │   │  │  • Uses: EBS Snapshots, RDS Snapshots, Velero     │    │
         │   │  │  • EKS: Velero backs up cluster + PVs to S3       │    │
         │   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │   │                                                           │
         │   └──────────────────────────────────────────────▶ RTO/RPO    │
         │      FASTER ────────────────────────────────▶ SLOWER          │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 anihpj RECOMMENDATION:                                     │
         │     Dev/Staging → Backup & Restore                            │
         │     Production → Warm Standby or Pilot Light                   │
         │     Mission-Critical → Multi-Site Active/Active               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    34.3 Disaster Recovery on AWS
         - RPO (Recovery Point Objective) vs RTO (Recovery Time Objective)
         - DR strategies: Backup & Restore → Pilot Light → Warm Standby → Multi-Site
         - EKS DR: Velero backup/restore, etcd snapshot to S3
         [DIAGRAM: DR spectrum — cost vs RTO/RPO for each strategy]
         [CODE: Velero backup schedule for anihpj cluster]

    34.4 Chaos Engineering on AWS
         - AWS Fault Injection Simulator (FIS)
         - Terminate instances, throttle network, exhaust CPU
         - GameDays: practice incident response
