Chapter 35: Root Cause Analysis & Post-Mortems                        🔴 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 5 (Incident Mgmt 14%) — RCA methodology is tested
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~2 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: 5 Whys RCA Technique ➜ Fishbone Diagram ➜
     AWS Toolchain for RCA (CloudTrail→X-Ray→Logs Insights) ➜ Post-Mortem
     Template ➜ Continuous Improvement Feedback Loop — 5 RCA visuals]


         [ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS — 5 WHYS TECHNIQUE APPLIED TO anihpj OUTAGE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔍 5 WHYS — DRILLING DOWN TO ROOT CAUSE                      │
         │                                                               │
         │  INCIDENT: anihpj /jobs/ endpoint returning 500 errors         │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  WHY #1: Why is /jobs/ returning 500 errors?         │    │
         │  │  ─────────────────────────────────────────            │    │
         │  │  → Because the database query timed out after 30s    │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  WHY #2: Why did the database query time out?        │    │
         │  │  ───────────────────────────────────────────          │    │
         │  │  → Because the jobpost table has 5M rows and no       │    │
         │  │    index on the "skills" column used in the WHERE     │    │
         │  │    clause                                              │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  WHY #3: Why is there no index on the skills column? │    │
         │  │  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────── │    │
         │  │  → Because the database migration to add the index    │    │
         │  │    was written but never applied in production        │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  WHY #4: Why was the migration not applied in prod?  │    │
         │  │  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────── │    │
         │  │  → Because the CI/CD pipeline deploys code but        │    │
         │  │    database migrations are run MANUALLY by a DBA      │    │
         │  │    who was on vacation                                  │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  WHY #5: Why are migrations manual and not automated? │    │
         │  │  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────── │    │
         │  │  → Because the team feared automated migrations       │    │
         │  │    would corrupt data. No rollback strategy existed.  │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  🎯 ROOT CAUSE:                                       │    │
         │  │  ─────────────                                         │    │
         │  │  Lack of automated, tested database migration         │    │
         │  │  pipeline with rollback capability.                   │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  ACTION ITEMS:                                                 │
         │  ─────────────                                                 │
         │  1. ✅ Add the missing index immediately                       │
         │  2. 🔧 Create a K8s Job for Django migrations in CI/CD         │
         │  3. 🔧 Add RDS snapshot before each migration (rollback)       │
         │  4. 🔧 Add CloudWatch Alarm for query duration > 1 second     │
         │  5. 📝 Update runbook: "Database performance degradation"     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    35.1 RCA Methodology
         - 5 Whys technique
         - Fishbone (Ishikawa) diagrams
         - Blameless post-mortem culture
         [TEMPLATE: Post-mortem document structure]

    35.2 RCA with AWS Tools
         - CloudTrail: "Who made that API call?"
         - CloudWatch Logs Insights: "What happened just before the crash?"
         - X-Ray: "Which service in the chain failed?"
         [EXAMPLE: anihpj outage — step-by-step RCA using AWS tools]

    35.3 Continuous Improvement
         - Incident → Post-Mortem → Action Items → Automation → Prevention
         - Feedback loop: every incident makes the system more resilient

Chapter 37: EKS Cost Optimization                                       🔴 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%) — EKS cost optimization for DevOps
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~2 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: K8s Cost Breakdown (Pie Chart) ➜ Spot+Savings Plans
     Strategy ➜ Kubecost Namespace View ➜ Fargate vs EC2 Cost Matrix — 4 visuals]


         [EKS COST BREAKDOWN — WHERE YOUR MONEY GOES]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  💰 TYPICAL EKS CLUSTER COST BREAKDOWN (anihpj Production)    │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │         ┌─────────────────────────┐                  │    │
         │  │         │     ☸️  EKS Control Plane  │  5% ($70/mo)  │    │
         │  │         │     $0.10/hr ≈ $72/mo    │                  │    │
         │  │         └─────────────────────────┘                  │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │  💻 COMPUTE (EC2/Fargate): ~70%                   │ │    │
         │  │  │  ─────────────────────────────                    │ │    │
         │  │  │  • 3 × t3.large On-Demand: $0.0832/hr × 3 × 730 │ │    │
         │  │  │    = $182/month (baseline)                        │ │    │
         │  │  │  • 2 × t3.large Spot: $0.025/hr × 2 × 730       │ │    │
         │  │  │    = $36/month (burst capacity)                   │ │    │
         │  │  │  • SAVINGS: Spot saves 70% vs On-Demand!         │ │    │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │  💾 STORAGE (EBS/EFS): ~15%                      │ │    │
         │  │  │  ──────────────────────────                       │ │    │
         │  │  │  • EBS gp3 (50 GB × 3 nodes): $12/month          │ │    │
         │  │  │  • EFS (20 GB shared): $6/month                  │ │    │
         │  │  │  • S3 (static files + backups): $4/month         │ │    │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │    │
         │  │  │  🌐 NETWORKING: ~10%                              │ │    │
         │  │  │  ────────────────────                              │ │    │
         │  │  │  • NAT Gateway ($0.045/hr): $33/month            │ │    │
         │  │  │  • ALB ($0.0225/hr): $16/month                   │ │    │
         │  │  │  • Data Transfer (100 GB out): $9/month          │ │    │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │    │
         │  │                                                       │    │
         │  │  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════  │    │
         │  │  TOTAL: ~$370/month (On-Demand baseline)              │    │
         │  │  WITH SPOT + SAVINGS PLANS: ~$220/month (40% saved!)  │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 COST OPTIMIZATION CHECKLIST:                               │
         │     ✅ Use Spot instances for stateless Pods (60-90% off)      │
         │     ✅ Compute Savings Plans for baseline (30% off On-Demand)  │
         │     ✅ Graviton (ARM) instances: 20% cheaper + 20% faster      │
         │     ✅ VPC Endpoints for S3 (avoid NAT Gateway data charges)   │
         │     ✅ Right-size Pod requests (don't over-provision)          │
         │     ✅ Delete idle resources (snapshots, unattached EBS, old   │
         │        ECR images)                                            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    37.1 Understanding K8s Costs
         - Compute (EC2/Fargate): ~70% of cluster cost
         - Storage (EBS/EFS): ~15%
         - Networking (NAT GW, LB, data transfer): ~10%
         - Management (EKS control plane): ~5%
         [PIE CHART: Typical EKS cost breakdown]

    37.2 Compute Optimization Strategies
         - Rightsizing Pods: set proper requests and limits
         - Spot Instances for stateless workloads (60-90% savings)
         - Savings Plans for baseline capacity
         - Graviton (ARM) instances: 20% cheaper, 20% faster
         - Cluster Autoscaler + Karpenter: remove idle nodes
         [CODE: Mixed instance policy + Spot for anihpj node group]

    37.3 Kubecost & Cost Visibility
         - Kubecost: open-source K8s cost monitoring
         - Cost by Namespace, Deployment, Pod, Label
         - Efficiency metrics: idle costs, overprovisioning
         [DIAGRAM: Kubecost dashboard — namespace-level cost breakdown]

    37.4 Network Cost Optimization
         - VPC endpoints (avoid NAT Gateway for S3/DynamoDB)
         - CloudFront caching (reduce origin traffic)
         - Inter-AZ traffic awareness (Pod anti-affinity)
         - PrivateLink for cross-account services

    37.5 Fargate vs EC2 for EKS — Cost Comparison
         [COMPARISON TABLE: Fargate vs EC2 for steady vs bursty workloads]
         | Scenario                    | Fargate Cost | EC2 Cost | Recommendation |
         | Steady 24/7 workload        | Higher       | Lower    | EC2 + Savings Plans |
         | Bursty/spiky workload       | Lower        | Higher   | Fargate |
         | Batch jobs                  | Lower        | Lower    | EC2 Spot |

    🏋️  Chapter 37 Practice Drill: Optimize anihpj Costs on EKS

Chapter 38B: AWS Kinesis — Real-Time Data Streaming                🔴 🎯
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    38B.1 Kinesis Data Streams
         - Real-time data ingestion (1000s sources → shards)
         - Shards, partition keys, sequence numbers
         - Consumers: Lambda, Kinesis Data Firehose, Kinesis Client Library (KCL)
         - Retention: 1-365 days
         - On-demand vs Provisioned mode
         [DIAGRAM: Kinesis Data Streams — Producers → Shards → Consumers]

    38B.2 Kinesis Data Firehose
         - Fully managed: auto-scaling, no shard management
         - Destinations: S3, Redshift, OpenSearch, Splunk, HTTP endpoints
         - Data transformation with Lambda
         - Buffer size & interval tuning
         [DIAGRAM: Firehose — Stream → Lambda Transform → S3/OpenSearch/Redshift]

    38B.3 Kinesis Data Analytics (Managed Flink)
         - SQL-based real-time analytics on streaming data
         - Apache Flink for advanced stream processing
         [USE CASE: Real-time analytics on jobpost application events]

    38B.4 SQS Dead Letter Queues & SNS Redrive
         - SQS DLQ: isolate failed messages after maxReceiveCount
         - DLQ redrive: move messages back to source queue
         - SNS redrive policy (DLQ for failed deliveries to subscribers)
         [DIAGRAM: SQS → DLQ → Redrive → Source Queue flow]

Chapter 39: Deployment Architecture Patterns                            🔴 🎯
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    🎯 AWS DEVOPS EXAM: Domain 3 (Resiliency 15%) — Architecture patterns for HA
    ⏱  Estimated Study Time: ~3 hours

    [DIAGRAMS IN THIS CHAPTER: Blue/Green Traffic Switch ➜ Multi-AZ Architecture
     ➜ Multi-Region Architecture ➜ DR Strategy Spectrum ➜ Auto Scaling Deep
     Dive ➜ Microservices Decomposition (Strangler Fig) — 6 architecture patterns]


         [BLUE/GREEN DEPLOYMENT — ROUTE 53 WEIGHTED ROUTING]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔵🟢 BLUE/GREEN AT THE DNS LEVEL — ZERO RISK TRAFFIC SWITCH  │
         │                                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  ROUTE 53 WEIGHTED ROUTING POLICY                     │    │
         │  │  ──────────────────────────────────                    │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  anihpj.example.com                                     │    │
         │  │       │                                                  │    │
         │  │       ├── Blue ALB  (weight: 100)  ← Current v1.0      │    │
         │  │       │   └── Blue EKS Cluster (us-east-1)             │    │
         │  │       │       └── Pods: v1.0 (3 replicas)              │    │
         │  │       │                                                  │    │
         │  │       └── Green ALB (weight: 0)    ← New v2.0           │    │
         │  │           └── Green EKS Cluster (us-west-2)             │    │
         │  │               └── Pods: v2.0 (3 replicas)               │    │
         │  │                                                          │    │
         │  │  TRAFFIC: 100% → Blue, 0% → Green                       │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                          │                                    │
         │                          │ After validation                    │
         │                          ▼                                    │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  ROUTE 53 WEIGHTED ROUTING POLICY (UPDATED)           │    │
         │  │  ─────────────────────────────────────────             │    │
         │  │                                                         │    │
         │  │  anihpj.example.com                                     │    │
         │  │       │                                                  │    │
         │  │       ├── Blue ALB  (weight: 0)   ← Decommissioned     │    │
         │  │       │                                                  │    │
         │  │       └── Green ALB (weight: 100) ← Now production!    │    │
         │  │                                                          │    │
         │  │  TRAFFIC: 0% → Blue, 100% → Green                       │    │
         │  │                                                          │    │
         │  │  🚨 ROLLBACK: Swap weights back (Blue: 100, Green: 0)   │    │
         │  │     Takes ~60 seconds for DNS propagation!              │    │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                               │
         │  💡 Blue/Green at DNS level enables CROSS-REGION deployment!  │
         │     Deploy Green in a DIFFERENT region for extra safety.      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    39.1  Blue/Green Architecture
          - Two identical environments, instant traffic switch
          - Route 53 weighted routing or ALB target group swap
          - Elastic Beanstalk environment swap
          [DIAGRAM: Blue/Green — Blue (current) → Green (new) → DNS swap]

    39.2  Multi-AZ Architecture
          - Auto Scaling Groups across 3+ AZs
          - RDS Multi-AZ (synchronous standby)
          - ElastiCache Multi-AZ (automatic failover)
          - ALB distributes across AZs, health checks

    39.3  Multi-Region Architecture
          - Route 53 Latency/Failover/Geolocation routing
          - S3 Cross-Region Replication (CRR)
          - DynamoDB Global Tables
          - RDS Cross-Region Read Replicas
          - EKS clusters per region + Global Accelerator
          [DIAGRAM: Multi-Region — us-east-1 (primary) + eu-west-1 (DR)]

    39.4  Disaster Recovery in AWS
          - RPO (Recovery Point Objective) vs RTO (Recovery Time Objective)
          - DR Strategies: Backup & Restore → Pilot Light → Warm Standby → Multi-Site Active/Active
          - DR with EKS: Velero backup to S3 cross-region
          - Aurora Global Database (RPO < 1 sec, RTO < 1 min)
          [DIAGRAM: DR spectrum — cost ↑ vs RTO/RPO ↓ for each strategy]

    39.5  Auto Scaling Deep Dive
          - EC2 Auto Scaling: Scaling Policies (Target Tracking, Step, Simple, Scheduled)
          - Lifecycle hooks (custom actions on launch/terminate)
          - Warm Pools (pre-initialized instances for faster scale-out)
          - Application Auto Scaling (ECS, DynamoDB, Aurora, Lambda, SageMaker)
          - ASG Termination Policies & Event Notifications


Chapter 39B: Microservices Architecture on EKS                       🔴 ☸️
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    39.1 Monolith to Microservices — The Journey
         - anihpj as a monolith: all-in-one Django app
         - Breaking it down: Auth service, Job service, User service, Notification service
         - Strangler Fig pattern: gradually replace monolith pieces
         [DIAGRAM: Monolith → Microservices evolution with Strangler Fig]

    39.2 Microservices Communication Patterns
         - Synchronous: REST/gRPC (Service-to-Service)
         - Asynchronous: SQS, SNS, EventBridge, Kinesis
         - Service discovery: K8s DNS, Cloud Map
         [DIAGRAM: Synchronous vs Asynchronous communication in EKS]

    39.3 API Gateway Patterns
         - API Gateway → EKS (via VPC Link/NLB)
         - Rate limiting, authentication, request transformation
         - API Gateway + Lambda authorizers

    39.4 Distributed Data Patterns
         - Database per service
         - Saga pattern (distributed transactions)
         - CQRS and Event Sourcing
         - Caching strategies (ElastiCache, DAX)

    🏋️  Chapter 39 Practice Drill: Design Microservices for anihpj

         - Serverless containers: no node management
         - Fargate on ECS vs Fargate on EKS

         - Fargate Spot (70% discount for fault-tolerant workloads)

    41.2 Lambda + EKS Integration Patterns

         - EKS → Lambda (offload compute-intensive tasks)
         - Event-driven patterns

         - Auto-scaling, HTTPS, load balancing built-in
         - App Runner vs ECS vs EKS vs Lambda

         │  │ "Canary deploy"     │  CodeDeploy + Lambda traffic shift│  │

         │  │                     │  kubectl rollout undo             │  │

         │  │ "Pod permissions"   │  IRSA (IAM Roles for Service Accts)│  │

         │  │                     │  (on-prem)                        │  │
         │  │ "Container registry"│  ECR                               │  │
         │  │ "Serverless code"   │  Lambda                            │  │
         │  │ "Serverless containers"│ Fargate                        │  │
         │  │ "Message queue"     │  SQS (decouple)                   │  │
         │  │ "Pub/sub messaging" │  SNS (fanout)                     │  │
         │  │ "Event-driven"      │  EventBridge                      │  │
         │  │ "Workflow/Orchest." │  Step Functions                   │  │
         │  │ "DNS routing"       │  Route 53 (Latency/Failover/Geo)  │  │
         │  │ "CDN/Cache"         │  CloudFront                       │  │
         │  │ "Cost optimization" │  Spot + Savings Plans + Graviton  │  │
         │  │ "Patch management"  │  SSM Patch Manager                │  │

         │  │ "TLS certificate"   │  ACM (AWS Certificate Manager)    │  │

         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    44.1 Quick Reference — AWS Services Map
         [COMPLETE SERVICE MAP: All 200+ AWS services organized by category]
         | Category          | Key Services                                      |
         | Compute           | EC2, ECS, EKS, Lambda, Fargate, App Runner       |
         | Containers        | ECR, EKS, ECS, Fargate                            |
         | CI/CD             | CodeCommit, CodeBuild, CodeDeploy, CodePipeline   |
         | IaC               | CloudFormation, CDK, Service Catalog, Proton      |
         | Monitoring        | CloudWatch, CloudTrail, Config, X-Ray             |
         | Security          | IAM, KMS, Secrets Mgr, GuardDuty, Security Hub    |
         | Networking        | VPC, ALB/NLB, Route 53, CloudFront, API GW       |
         | Messaging         | SNS, SQS, EventBridge, Step Functions            |
         | Storage           | S3, EBS, EFS, FSx                                 |
         | Database          | RDS, DynamoDB, ElastiCache, Aurora               |
         | Management        | SSM, Organizations, Control Tower                |

    44.2 Top 50 Must-Know Facts for AWS DevOps
         [FLASHCARD FORMAT: Quick-fire facts]
         1. EKS control plane runs in AWS-managed VPC (not yours)
         2. IRSA uses OIDC to give Pods IAM roles
         3. CodeDeploy can do Blue/Green on EKS with ALB
         4. CloudFormation Drift Detection finds manual changes
         5. EventBridge can replay events (archive required)
         ... (50 facts total)

    44.3 Common Scenario Patterns
         - "High availability" → Multi-AZ, ASG, Route 53 failover
         - "Cost optimization" → Spot, Savings Plans, Graviton, S3 Lifecycle
         - "Security" → IAM least privilege, KMS encryption, GuardDuty
         - "Performance" → CloudFront, ElastiCache, Read Replicas
         - "Disaster Recovery" → Multi-Region, RDS backups, Velero for EKS

    44.4 Final Confidence Check
         - Self-assessment scorecard
         - Weak area identification → revisit specific chapters
         - "Am I ready?" checklist

Appendix A: kubectl Command Quick Reference
───────────────────────────────────────────
    A.1  Cluster Info Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl cluster-info          # API server + CoreDNS endpoints │
         │ kubectl version --short       # Client + Server versions       │
         │ kubectl get nodes             # List all nodes                 │
         │ kubectl get nodes -o wide     # + IP, OS, kernel, runtime      │
         │ kubectl top nodes             # CPU/Memory usage per node      │
         │ kubectl get namespaces        # List all namespaces            │
         │ kubectl get all -n production # Everything in a namespace      │
         │ kubectl api-resources         # All available resource types   │
         │ kubectl explain pod.spec      # Built-in field documentation   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.2  Pod Management Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl get pods                          # All pods (default ns)│
         │ kubectl get pods -n production            # Pods in namespace   │
         │ kubectl get pods -o wide                  # + IP, Node, status  │
         │ kubectl describe pod anihpj-7d4f-abcde    # Full pod details    │
         │ kubectl logs anihpj-7d4f-abcde            # Pod logs (stdout)   │
         │ kubectl logs -f anihpj-7d4f-abcde         # Stream logs (follow)│
         │ kubectl logs --tail=50 anihpj-7d4f-abcde  # Last 50 lines       │
         │ kubectl logs -l app=anihpj --all-containers # All matching      │
         │ kubectl exec -it anihpj-7d4f-abcde -- bash # Shell into pod     │
         │ kubectl exec anihpj-7d4f-abcde -- env       # Print env vars    │
         │ kubectl port-forward pod/anihpj-7d4f 8080:8000 # Local→Pod port │
         │ kubectl delete pod anihpj-7d4f-abcde     # Delete (recreated!)  │
         │ kubectl delete pod --force --grace-period=0  # Force delete     │
         │ kubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp' # Recent events   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.3  Deployment Management Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl get deployments -n production    # List deployments     │
         │ kubectl describe deployment anihpj       # Full deploy details  │
         │ kubectl scale deployment anihpj --replicas=5  # Manual scale    │
         │ kubectl rollout status deployment/anihpj # Watch rollout        │
         │ kubectl rollout history deployment/anihpj # Revision history    │
         │ kubectl rollout undo deployment/anihpj   # Rollback to prev     │
         │ kubectl rollout undo deployment/anihpj --to-revision=2 # Specific│
         │ kubectl set image deploy/anihpj anihpj=anihpj:v2 # Update image │
         │ kubectl edit deployment anihpj            # Live edit YAML      │
         │ kubectl patch deployment anihpj -p '{"spec":{"replicas":5}}'  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.4  Service & Networking Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl get svc -n production             # List services       │
         │ kubectl describe svc anihpj-service        # Endpoints, ports   │
         │ kubectl get endpoints anihpj-service       # Pod IPs behind svc │
         │ kubectl get ingress -n production          # List ingress rules │
         │ kubectl get networkpolicies                # Network policies   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.5  ConfigMap & Secret Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl create configmap app-config --from-literal=LOG=info   │
         │ kubectl create configmap app-config --from-file=config.json   │
         │ kubectl get configmap app-config -o yaml    # View config      │
         │ kubectl create secret generic db-secret \                     │
         │   --from-literal=password=MyP@ssw0rd                          │
         │ kubectl create secret docker-registry ecr-secret \            │
         │   --docker-server=123.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com \       │
         │   --docker-username=AWS --docker-password=$(aws ecr get-login)│
         │ kubectl get secrets                        # List secrets      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.6  Debugging & Troubleshooting Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl describe pod anihpj-7d4f-abcde   # Events at bottom    │
         │ kubectl get events --field-selector involvedObject.name=pod-X │
         │ kubectl top pods -n production            # Resource usage      │
         │ kubectl debug -it anihpj-7d4f --image=busybox --target=anihpj │
         │ kubectl cp anihpj-7d4f:/app/logs ./local-logs  # Copy files    │
         │ kubectl auth can-i create deployments --as=system:serviceaccount│
         │ kubectl get pods --field-selector=status.phase=Failed         │
         │ kubectl get pods -o json | jq '.items[]|{name:.metadata.name}'│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.7  Node Management Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl cordon node-1         # Mark unschedulable (no new pods)│
         │ kubectl drain node-1 --ignore-daemonsets  # Evacuate + cordon  │
         │ kubectl uncordon node-1       # Mark schedulable again          │
         │ kubectl taint nodes node-1 key=value:NoSchedule  # Add taint   │
         │ kubectl taint nodes node-1 key=value:NoSchedule- # Remove      │
         │ kubectl label nodes node-1 disk=ssd     # Add label            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.8  YAML Generation (Imperative → Declarative Shortcuts)
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ export do="--dry-run=client -o yaml"  # MUST set this alias!   │
         │                                                                │
         │ kubectl create deploy anihpj --image=anihpj:v1 $do > deploy.yaml│
         │ kubectl create deploy web --image=nginx --replicas=3 $do      │
         │ kubectl expose deploy anihpj --port=80 --target-port=8000 $do │
         │ kubectl create configmap cfg --from-literal=k=v $do           │
         │ kubectl create secret generic sec --from-literal=k=v $do      │
         │ kubectl create job myjob --image=busybox -- /bin/sh -c "date" $do│
         │ kubectl create cronjob mycron --image=busybox --schedule="*/5" │
         │   -- /bin/sh -c "date" $do                                     │
         │ kubectl run nginx --image=nginx $do > pod.yaml                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.9  kubectl JSONPath & Custom Columns
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # Get pod IPs with custom columns                               │
         │ kubectl get pods -o=custom-columns=NAME:.metadata.name,IP:.status.podIP│
         │                                                                │
         │ # JSONPath examples                                             │
         │ kubectl get nodes -o jsonpath='{.items[*].status.addresses[?(@.type=="InternalIP")].address}'│
         │ kubectl get pods -o jsonpath='{.items[?(@.status.phase=="Running")].metadata.name}'│
         │                                                                │
         │ # Sort pods by restart count                                    │
         │ kubectl get pods --sort-by='.status.containerStatuses[0].restartCount'│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix B: Essential kubectl JSONPath Patterns
───────────────────────────────────────────────
    B.1  Basic JSONPath Syntax
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ {.items[*].metadata.name}          # All item names            │
         │ {.items[0].metadata.name}          # First item's name         │
         │ {.items[?(@.status.phase=="Running")].metadata.name}  # Filter│
         │ {.items[*].metadata.labels.app}     # Specific label value     │
         │ {range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}{"\t"}{.status.podIP}{"\n"}{end}│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.2  Common Query Patterns
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # All pod names and their node assignments                      │
         │ kubectl get pods -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}{"\t"}{.spec.nodeName}{"\n"}{end}'│
         │                                                                  │
         │ # All images running in all pods                                │
         │ kubectl get pods -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.spec.containers[*].image}{"\n"}{end}'│
         │                                                                  │
         │ # Service ClusterIPs                                             │
         │ kubectl get svc -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}{"\t"}{.spec.clusterIP}{"\n"}{end}'│
         │                                                                  │
         │ # PVC storage requests                                            │
         │ kubectl get pvc -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}{"\t"}{.spec.resources.requests.storage}{"\n"}{end}'│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix C: Docker Commands for DevOps Engineers
────────────────────────────────────────────────
    C.1  Image Management
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ docker build -t anihpj:v1 .        # Build from Dockerfile     │
         │ docker build --no-cache -t anihpj:v1 .  # Fresh build          │
         │ docker images                      # List local images          │
         │ docker rmi anihpj:v1               # Remove image               │
         │ docker tag anihpj:v1 123.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1│
         │ docker history anihpj:v1           # Show image layers          │
         │ docker inspect anihpj:v1           # Full image metadata        │
         │ docker image prune -a              # Remove ALL unused images   │
         │ docker save -o anihpj.tar anihpj:v1  # Export to tar            │
         │ docker load -i anihpj.tar          # Import from tar            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    C.2  Container Management
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ docker run -d -p 8000:8000 --name anihpj-prod anihpj:v1       │
         │ docker run -d --restart=always anihpj:v1  # Auto-restart       │
         │ docker ps                          # Running containers         │
         │ docker ps -a                       # All containers (incl stopped)│
         │ docker logs -f anihpj-prod         # Stream logs                │
         │ docker exec -it anihpj-prod bash   # Shell into container       │
         │ docker stop anihpj-prod            # Graceful stop              │
         │ docker rm anihpj-prod              # Remove container            │
         │ docker stats                       # Live resource usage         │
         │ docker top anihpj-prod             # Running processes           │
         │ docker cp anihpj-prod:/app/logs ./ # Copy files from container  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    C.3  ECR-Specific Commands
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # Authenticate Docker to ECR                                    │
         │ aws ecr get-login-password --region us-east-1 | \              │
         │   docker login --username AWS --password-stdin \               │
         │   123456789012.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com                 │
         │                                                                  │
         │ # Create ECR repository                                         │
         │ aws ecr create-repository --repository-name anihpj \           │
         │   --image-scanning-configuration scanOnPush=true               │
         │                                                                  │
         │ # Push image                                                    │
         │ docker tag anihpj:v1 123.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1│
         │ docker push 123.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1      │
         │                                                                  │
         │ # Pull image                                                    │
         │ docker pull 123.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1      │
         │                                                                  │
         │ # List images in ECR                                            │
         │ aws ecr list-images --repository-name anihpj                   │
         │ aws ecr describe-images --repository-name anihpj               │
         │                                                                  │
         │ # Start vulnerability scan                                      │
         │ aws ecr start-image-scan --repository-name anihpj \            │
         │   --image-id imageTag=v1                                        │
         │ aws ecr describe-image-scan-findings --repository-name anihpj \│
         │   --image-id imageTag=v1                                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    C.4  Dockerfile Best Practices (Quick Reference)
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ ✅ DO:                             ❌ DON'T:                    │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────── │
         │ Use specific tags (python:3.12)    Use :latest (unpredictable) │
         │ Multi-stage builds                 Single bloated image         │
         │ COPY requirements.txt FIRST        COPY . first (breaks cache) │
         │ Non-root USER                      USER root (security risk)   │
         │ .dockerignore file                 Include .git, venv, *.pyc   │
         │ HEALTHCHECK instruction            No health checks             │
         │ Combine RUN commands (&& \)        Separate RUN (more layers)  │
         │ Clean apt cache (rm -rf /var/...)  Leave build deps in image   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix D: AWS CLI Commands for DevOps
────────────────────────────────────────
    D.1  EKS & kubectl Integration
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # Update kubeconfig for EKS cluster                             │
